### Analysis Steps

**0. Triage - Determine Strategy**
*   **Strategy:** Path 1 (User Request). The user requests a solution to the Inviscid Burgers' equation with Periodic BCs.
*   **Advisor Report:** None.
*   **Data Role:** Condition B (Validation Data). Role: Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.

**1. Data Source**
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Instruction:** Load this file for validation. Keys are `t`, `x`, `usol`.

**2. Problem Statement**
*   **Governing Equation:** Inviscid Burgers' Equation.
    *   Residual: $f = u_t + u \cdot u_x$
*   **Boundary/Initial Conditions:**
    *   **IC:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$ (where $\pi \approx 3.14159$).
    *   **BCs:** Periodic in $x$. $u(-1, t) = u(1, t)$.
*   **Analytical Solution:** Reference solution provided in data file.
*   **Data Loss Term:** No.
*   **Problem Type:** Forward.
*   **Well-posedness:** The problem admits weak solutions (shocks). Standard PINNs may struggle, but we proceed as requested.

**2.1. Input**
*   Domain dimension (d): 1D ($x$) + time ($t$).

**2.2. Output**
*   Value dimension (q): Scalar ($u$).
*   Characteristics: Periodic, develops shocks (high frequency).

**3. Base Model Selection**
*   **Selection:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.
*   **Reasoning:** Problem is 1D+t (Case A).

**4. Main Architecture**
*   **Structure:** Input -> Input Transformation -> Main Body -> Output Transformation -> Output.
*   **Hyperparameters:** `width=30`, `depth=3`.

**4.1. Input Transformations**
*   **Normalization:** Normalize $x$ to $[-1, 1]$ and $t$ to $[0, 1]$ inside the model.
*   **Feature Embeddings:** **Option A: Periodic Fourier Features**.
    *   **Degree:** Set `degree=2` (Global Stability Constraint).
    *   **Input Composition Rule:** Pass **ONLY** the embeddings of $x$ and the raw normalized $t$ to the network. **DO NOT** pass raw $x$.

**4.2. Main Body Architecture**
*   **Specs:** Base MLP (`width=30`, `depth=3`).
*   **Optimizer:** Hybrid Adam + SSBroyden.

**4.3. Output Transformation**
*   **Transformation:** `u_pred = NN(t, features(x))`
*   **Reasoning:** Periodicity is handled by the input embedding. No specific ansatz required for IC (soft constraint).

**5. Constraint Implementation Strategy**
*   **Count:** `L_pde` (1) + `L_ic` (1) = 2 terms. (Periodic BCs handled architecturally).
*   **Decision:** Count < 3. Use **Loss-Based** strategy for IC.

**6. Loss Function Assembly**
*   **Symbolic Equation:** $L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{ic}$
*   **Components:**
    *   `L_pde`: Residual of $u_t + u u_x$.
    *   `L_ic`: MSE against $-\sin(\pi x)$ at $t=0$.
    *   `L_bc_periodic`: **EXCLUDED** (Satisfied by architecture).

**6.1. Continuation Strategy**
*   **Status:** None. The problem is inviscid (no viscosity parameter to anneal).

**7. Weighting Strategy**
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for `L_pde`. Used for Weighting & Sampling during Adam, and Sampling ONLY during SSBroyden.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Total Terms:** 2 (`L_pde`, `L_ic`).
    *   **Strategy:** **Self-Balancing Strategy**.
    *   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamic updates.
    *   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** global weights at final Adam values.

**8. Visualization Strategy**
*   Standard visualization. Do not modify template code.

**9. Saving Data**
*   Save `u_pred` as `.npz` with keys `t`, `x`, `u_pred`.

**Main Model:**
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]

**Enhancements:**
[Periodic Fourier Features]

--- Critic report---
[REJECTED]

**Critique:** Physics Logic – Zero Viscosity / Shock Trap. The target PDE is inviscid Burgers (effectively Re → ∞, shocks form), yet the plan uses a pure strong-form residual with **no** viscosity regularization, **no** viscosity continuation schedule, and **no** entropy penalty. This directly violates the “Zero Viscosity” rule: for shock-forming PDEs you cannot train a standard PINN on the inviscid form alone.

**Requirement:** Reformulate the strategy to solve a *viscous* surrogate problem with a small but finite viscosity ν, combined with continuation and entropy regularization. Concretely:
- Add a viscosity term: solve \(u_t + u u_x = \nu u_{xx}\) with ν > 0.
- Design a **continuation schedule** on ν: e.g., start at a relatively large ν (consistent with an effective Re ≈ 100) and **stepwise** decrease ν across at least 5 stages down to a small ν ≈ 10⁻⁴, with monotonically increasing iteration counts and ≥25k iterations at the final/smallest-ν stage.
- Optionally include an **entropy penalty** (e.g., enforcing Lax entropy condition or a monotone flux-based surrogate) to select the physically admissible weak solution.
- Keep the strong-form PINN framework, but now with the viscous PDE residual and the required continuation; do **not** revert to ν = 0 in training.
Once ν-regularization + continuation (and, ideally, entropy penalty) are explicitly integrated, the rest of the plan (data path, variables, optimizer strategy, vRBA usage, periodic handling) can be reused.

*   **Components:**
    *   `L_pde`: Residual of $u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx}$.
    *   `L_ic`: MSE against $-\sin(\pi x)$ at $t=0$.
    *   `L_bc_periodic`: **EXCLUDED** (Satisfied by architecture).

**6.1. Continuation Strategy (Viscosity Annealing)**
*   **Context:** Anneal $\nu$ to approach the inviscid limit.
*   **Parameter:** `nu` (passed to Loss Function).
*   **Phase 1: Adam (Stabilization)**
    *   **State:** Static $\nu = 0.02$ (approx Re=100).
    *   **Iterations:** 5,000.
*   **Phase 2: SSBroyden (Continuation)**
    *   **State:** Dynamic. Step `nu` sequentially.
    *   **Schedule:**
        1.  $\nu = 0.01$ (Re=200). Dwell: 5,000 iters.
        2.  $\nu = 0.005$ (Re=400). Dwell: 10,000 iters.
        3.  $\nu = 0.001$ (Re=2000). Dwell: 15,000 iters.
        4.  $\nu = 0.0005$ (Re=4000). Dwell: 20,000 iters.
        5.  $\nu = 0.0001$ (Re=20000). Dwell: 25,000 iters.
    *   **Critical:** Instruct Planner to **reinitialize the SSBroyden Hessian** at the start of each new $\nu$ step.

**7. Weighting Strategy**

*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Total Terms:** 2.
    *   **Strategy:** **Self-Balancing Strategy**.
    *   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamic updates.
    *   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** global weights at final Adam values.

--- Critic report---
[REJECTED]

**Critique:** Reynolds/Viskosity Ladder – Continuation Bounds Violated. The continuation schedule on ν never reaches the mandated inviscid-approximation regime: the smallest ν is 1e-4, which corresponds to Re ≈ 1e4, but the rules require targeting **Re ≥ 1e4** *and* explicitly state that the **target viscosity cannot be greater than 1e-4**. Here, ν_target = 1e-4 exactly, not below; this undershoots the required limit (should reach ν ≤ 1e-4).  

**Requirement:** Adjust the continuation so that:
- The **final stage** uses ν_final strictly **≤ 1e-4** (e.g., 5e-5 or 1e-5), while keeping the stepwise, piecewise-constant ν values and monotonically increasing dwell times (with ≥25,000 iterations at the final step).
- Keep the existing structure (start ν=0.02, then 0.01, 0.005, 0.001, 5e-4), but add at least one more step to reach ν_final ≤ 1e-4 (e.g., ν=1e-4 then ν=5e-5), or shift the current last step from 1e-4 to a smaller value such as 5e-5 with ≥25k iterations.

[REJECTED]

**Critique:** Reynolds/Viscosity Ladder – Fake Continuation Start. The continuation schedule violates the rule that **the minimum continuation parameter should be Re ≈ 100**, i.e., starting viscosity should be around ν ≈ 10⁻²; here, Phase 1 already uses ν = 0.02 (≈ Re 50), which is **below** the mandated starting Reynolds and explicitly disallowed (“CRITICAL REJECT IF THE CONTINOUATION PLAN PROPOSES STARTING WITH Re<100”).  

**Requirement:** Raise the initial viscosity level to correspond to Re ≈ 100 (ν ≈ 0.01) and adjust the schedule accordingly. For example:
- Phase 1 (Adam): ν = 0.01, 5,000 iters.
- Phase 2 (SSBroyden): discrete ν steps like 0.005, 0.001, 5e-4, 1e-4, 5e-5, ... with monotonically increasing dwell times and ≥25,000 iterations at the final, smallest-ν step.
Keep ν_final ≤ 1e-4 and preserve Hessian reinitialization at each ν step, but **do not** use ν > 0.01 (Re < 100) as part of the continuation.

# CODE ANALYSIS (PART 1 of 14)
# FUNCTION: None
# PURPOSE: Unknown
# =======================================================
# =======================================================
---------------------------------------------------

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `Periodic_Fourier_Features` class to create a deterministic Fourier series expansion of the input coordinates. This is used to enforce periodic boundary conditions architecturally. This class is a standalone feature embedding layer and has no dependencies on other custom classes within the provided library.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

* **New Code (lines 40-42):**
    ```python
    def __call__(self, t: Array, x: Array) -> Array:
        # Normalize inputs
        t_norm = 2.0 * (t - self.lb[0]) / (self.ub[0] - self.lb[0]) - 1.0
        x_norm = 2.0 * (x - self.lb[1]) / (self.ub[1] - self.lb[1]) - 1.0
        
        x_embedded = self.enbedding(x_norm)
        X_concat = jnp.concatenate([t_norm, x_embedded], axis=-1)
    ```

--- Analizing: # ## Cell 7: PDE Residual Definition ---
  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
### [CURRENT_CELL_ID]
* **Action:** Replace the `Allen_Cahn` function with the `Viscous_Burgers` residual function. The new function calculates the residual $f = u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx}$ and accepts the viscosity `nu` as an argument, as required by the scientific strategy.
* **New Code (lines 3-9):**
    ```python
    def Viscous_Burgers(params, t, x, nu, apply_fn):
        u = apply_fn(params, t, x)
        v_t = jnp.ones_like(t)
        v_x = jnp.ones_like(x)
        
        # First derivatives using jvp
        ut = jvp(lambda t_val: apply_fn(params, t_val, x), (t,), (v_t,))[1]
        ux = jvp(lambda x_val: apply_fn(params, t, x_val), (x,), (v_x,))[1]
        
        # Second derivative using hvp (Hessian-vector product)
        # This requires a function like hvp_fwdfwd from the project's layers
        from Crunch.Models.layers import hvp_fwdfwd
        uxx = hvp_fwdfwd(lambda x_val: apply_fn(params, t, x_val), (x,), (v_x,))
        
        return ut + u * ux - nu * uxx
    ```
* **Action:** Update the `PDE_residuals` alias to point to the new `Viscous_Burgers` function.
* **New Code (line 11):**
    ```python
    PDE_residuals=Viscous_Burgers
    ```

--- Analizing: # ## Cell 18: Main SSBroyden Training Loop ---
  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
### [CURRENT_CELL_ID]
* **Action:** Implement the viscosity annealing schedule by wrapping the existing SSBroyden loop in an outer loop that iterates through the prescribed `nu` values and their corresponding dwell times.
* **New Code (lines 1-38):**
    ```python
    # Define the continuation schedule
    continuation_schedule = [
        {'nu': 0.005, 'dwell': 5000},
        {'nu': 0.001, 'dwell': 10000},
        {'nu': 0.0005, 'dwell': 15000},
        {'nu': 0.0001, 'dwell': 20000},
        {'nu': 0.00005, 'dwell': 25000},
    ]
    
    ## SSBroyden Hyperparameters
    static_options = static_options_SSBroyden
    static_options['maxiter'] = Nchange
    static_options_tuple = tuple(static_options.items())
    
    # Training
    effective_steps = 0
    Adam_Steps = all_its[-1]
    
    # Outer loop for viscosity annealing
    for step_info in continuation_schedule:
        nu = step_info['nu']
        dwell_time = step_info['dwell']
        num_outer_iterations = dwell_time // Nchange
    
        # Reinitialize Hessian at the start of each new nu step
        H0 = jnp.eye(len(initial_weights), dtype=jnp.float64)
        print(f"\nStarting continuation step with nu = {nu}, dwell time = {dwell_time} iterations.")
        
        pbar = tqdm(range(num_outer_iterations), desc=f"SSBroyden Training (nu={nu:.1e})")
        for it in pbar:
            initial_weights, H0, key, loss_val, nit, lambdas = SSBroyden_step(
                initial_weights, H0, key,
                tc, xc, lambdas,
                apply_fn, unflatten_func, batch_size, gamma, eta, k_samp_bfgs, c_samp_bfgs,
                static_options_tuple, it, nu, lamB,
                ti, xi, ui
            )
            effective_steps += nit
            current_params_for_eval = unflatten_func(initial_weights)
            u_pred_it = apply_fn(current_params_for_eval, t, x)
            error = relative_l2(u_pred_it, u_gt)
            error_vec = u_pred_it - u_gt
            L_infty = scipy.linalg.norm(error_vec.flatten(), ord=np.inf)
            L_2 = scipy.linalg.norm(error_vec.flatten(), ord=2)
            all_errors.append(error)
            all_loss.append(loss_val)
            all_its.append(effective_steps + Adam_Steps)
            all_L_infty.append(L_infty)
            all_L_2.append(L_2)
            pbar.set_postfix({
                'It': effective_steps,
                'Loss': f'{loss_val:.3e}',
                'RL2': f'{float(error):.3e}',
                'nu': f'{nu:.1e}',
                'lam_max': f'{jnp.max(lambdas):.3e}',
            })
    ```

--- 6. Reconstructing Code ---
--- 4. Calling Debugging Team ---
Inspector summary:
 Architecture is MLP (depth=3, width=30 per layer) with input transformation: normalization of t and x to [-1,1] inside forward, Periodic Fourier Features (kmax=degree=2, Lx=2.0) applied only to normalized x (concatenated with normalized t, no raw x passed). Output is direct scalar u. Optimizer workflow: Phase 1 Adam (5000 epochs/iterations, nu=0.01 fixed, batch_size=20000) using vRBA (exponential potential, phi=1.0, c_log=1.0) for local weighting inside L_pde and importance sampling, self-balancing global weights (lamE=1 fixed, dynamic lamB via EMA of PDE/BCs gradient norms with gamma_grads=0.99, alpha=1.0). Phase 2 SSBroyden continuation with exact schedule (nu=0.005 dwell=5000 iters via 50 outer loops of Nchange=100 steps each; nu=0.001 dwell=10000; nu=0.0005 dwell=15000; nu=0.0001 dwell=20000; nu=5e-05 dwell=25000), Hessian reinitialized to identity at each nu step start, vRBA lambdas updated for sampling only (k_samp_bfgs=1.0, c_samp_bfgs=0.0), global lamB frozen from Adam end. Loss terms: L_total = L_pde (mean((new_lambdas * residuals)^2) in Adam, plain mean(residuals^2) in SSBroyden) + lamB * L_ic (MSE vs -sin(pi x) at t=0), no BC or data terms.

    ```python
    updated_lambdas = update_lambdas(apply_fn, params_it, lambdas, gamma, eta, tc, xc, it, nu)
    _ , current_train_data_tuple = sample_points_pdf(
        subkey, batch_size, updated_lambdas, tc, xc, ti, xi, ui, k, c
    )
    # 4. Prepare the full options dictionary for minimize
    current_options = dict(static_options)
    current_options['initial_H'] = H0
    # 5. Run the SSBroyden optimization for Nchange steps
    result = minimize(
        fun=loss_and_gradient,
        x0=initial_weights,
        args=(apply_fn, unflatten_func, *current_train_data_tuple, nu, lamB),
        method='BFGS', # SSBroyden version of BFGs
        options=current_options)
    # 6. Recycle the Hessian purely
    new_H0 = result.hess_inv
    new_H0 = (new_H0 + jnp.transpose(new_H0)) / 2
    try:
        L = jnp.linalg.cholesky(new_H0)
        is_failed = jnp.any(jnp.isnan(L))
        final_H0 = jax.lax.cond(
            is_failed,
            lambda op: jnp.eye(op.shape[0], dtype=op.dtype),
            lambda op: op,
            operand=new_H0)
    except LinAlgError:
        final_H0 = jnp.eye(new_H0.shape[0], dtype=new_H0.dtype)
    return result.x, final_H0, key, result.fun, result.nit, updated_lambdas
    ```

```
File "...Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX.py", line 558
    r_i_norm = lax.stop_gradient(jnp.abs(residuals))
                                                    ^
IndentationError: unindent does not match any outer indentation level
```

**Root Cause:**
Inspecting Cell 16 (Lines 534-564), the function `update_lambdas` contains a nested function `get_lambdas` (Line 555). At Line 557, there is a comment `# Update lambdas` that is **incorrectly indented**. This comment is at the same indentation level as the function body of `get_lambdas`, but Line 558 (`r_i_norm = lax.stop_gradient(...)`) is indented one level deeper, as if it were inside a block that doesn't exist. Python interprets this as a mismatch.

**The Bug:**
Line 557 should either:
1. Be indented to match Line 558 (if it's a comment inside the function logic), OR
2. Line 558 should be unindented to align with Line 556 (the `residuals = ...` line).

The most likely intent is that Line 557 is a comment describing Lines 558-561, so **Line 557 should be indented to the same level as Line 558** (i.e., one more level of indentation than it currently has).

**Corrective Action for Planner:**
- In Cell 16, Line 557, add 4 spaces (or 1 tab) to the comment `# Update lambdas` so it aligns with the code block starting at Line 558.
- Alternatively, remove the comment entirely if it's redundant.

**Example Fix:**
```python
def get_lambdas(params, lambdas):
    residuals = PDE_residuals(params, t, x, nu, apply_fn)
    # Update lambdas  # <-- This line needs to be indented here
    r_i_norm = lax.stop_gradient(jnp.abs(residuals))
    new_lambdas = update_weights_fn(r_i_norm, lambdas, it, gamma=gamma, eta=eta)
    return new_lambdas
```

    ```python
    def update_lambdas(apply_fn,params,lambdas,gamma,eta,tc,xc,it, nu):
        t,x=tc,xc
        def get_lambdas(params, lambdas):
            residuals = PDE_residuals(params, t, x, nu, apply_fn)
            # Update lambdas
            r_i_norm = lax.stop_gradient(jnp.abs(residuals))
            new_lambdas = update_weights_fn(r_i_norm,lambdas, it,
                                              gamma=gamma,
                                              eta=eta)
            return new_lambdas
        new_lambdas=get_lambdas(params, lambdas)
        return new_lambdas
    ```

**Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback**
*   **Observation:**
    1.  **Loss & Error History:** The training exhibits a clear multi-stage continuation process. The model achieves a very low relative L2 error of approximately $3 \times 10^{-3}$ at iteration 20,000 (corresponding to $\nu=0.001$). However, in subsequent stages where viscosity is further decreased, the training becomes unstable. Around iteration 35,000, the error catastrophically jumps by two orders of magnitude and stagnates at a high value of ~0.2 for the remainder of the run. The final segment of the training is a flat plateau at this high error.
    2.  **Residual/Pointwise Error:** The error is sharply concentrated along the shock front developing at $x=0$. This indicates the model's primary failure is in accurately capturing the near-discontinuity.
    3.  **Solution Fields:** While the overall shape of the solution is correct, the predicted shock front is visibly more diffuse (smeared) than the sharp reference solution, which is consistent with the high final error and the failure to reach a very low viscosity.

*   **Diagnosis:** **Continuation Failure due to Missing Entropy Regularization.** The model successfully solved the viscous problem at moderate viscosity. As the continuation schedule reduced the artificial viscosity ($\nu$) to approach the inviscid limit, the problem became too stiff. The developing shock could not be resolved, leading to instability and a jump to a high-error solution. Using artificial viscosity is a valid strategy, but for strongly hyperbolic problems, a more physically-grounded regularization is required to select the correct "weak solution".

*   **Targeted Improvements:**

    *   **For Integrity:** None
    *   **For Accuracy:** The primary issue is the unstable handling of the shock in the low-viscosity limit. The current artificial viscosity approach failed. A more robust method is to enforce the physical entropy condition directly.
        **Recommendation:** **Impose an Entropy Penalty.**
        1.  Formulate the PDE residual using the **conservative form** of the Burgers' equation: $L_{pde} = u_t + \frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\frac{1}{2}u^2)$.
        2.  Add an **entropy inequality loss term** to the total loss: $L_{ent} = \sum \text{ReLU}(-\eta_t - q_x)^2$, where the entropy pair for Burgers' is $(\eta, q) = (u^2/2, u^3/3)$. This penalizes non-physical solutions and provides the necessary regularization to capture the shock robustly.
    *   **For Details:** The model failed to converge to an acceptable solution, so detailed refinements are secondary. The primary accuracy issue must be addressed first.
    *   **For Optimality:**
        *   *Constraint Formulation:* None
        *   *Output Transforms:* None
        *   *Entropy (FOR SHOCKS - CRITICAL):* For inviscid/low-viscosity problems with shocks (Burgers $\nu \le 1e-3$, Euler), impose Entropy Penalty $(\eta, q)$ and use conservative form.

### 1. Data Source
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.

### 2. Problem Statement
*   **Governing Equation:** Inviscid Burgers' Equation (Conservative Form).
    *   Residual: $r = u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2)$
    *   (Note: $u_t + u u_x = 0$ is the non-conservative form; Advisor mandates conservative).
*   **Entropy Condition:**
    *   Entropy Pair: $\eta(u) = 0.5 u^2$, $q(u) = u^3/3$.
    *   Physical Condition: $\eta_t + q_x \le 0$ (Entropy must dissipate at shocks).
    *   Loss Term: $L_{ent} = \text{ReLU}(\eta_t + q_x)^2$.
*   **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
*   **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
*   **IC:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
*   **Problem Type:** Forward.
*   **Well-posedness:** Hyperbolic conservation law with shocks. Requires Entropy condition for uniqueness.

### 2.1 Input
*   **Domain:** 1D space + time ($d=2$).

### 2.2 Output
*   **Value:** Scalar $u(t,x)$.
*   **Characteristics:** Shock formation expected.

### 3. Base Model Selection
*   **Type:** Low Dim (1D+t).
*   **Optimizer:** Hybrid Adam + SSBroyden.

### 4. Main Architecture
*   **Structure:** Input $\rightarrow$ Normalization $\rightarrow$ Embeddings $\rightarrow$ MLP $\rightarrow$ Output Transform $\rightarrow$ Output.

**4.1 Input Transformations**
*   **Normalization:** Normalize $x$ to $[-1, 1]$ and $t$ to $[0, 1]$.
*   **Feature Embeddings:**
    *   **Type:** **Periodic Fourier Features** (Option A) for $x$ (matches Periodic BCs).
    *   **Configuration:** `degree = 2` (Start low as per stability constraint).
    *   **Composition:** Pass **ONLY** the Fourier embeddings of $x$ and the raw normalized $t$ to the MLP. Do **NOT** pass raw $x$.

**4.2 Main Body Architecture**
*   **Specs:** `width=30`, `depth=3` (Base MLP for Low Dim).
*   **Optimizer Strategy:**
    *   **Phase 1 (Adam):** 10,000 iterations. Dynamic Self-Balancing Weights.
    *   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** 10,000 iterations. **Frozen** Global Weights.

**4.3 Output Transformation (Hard Constraints)**
*   **Strategy:** Hard Constraints for IC and Periodic BCs.
*   **Ansatz:**
    $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, \text{features}(x)) $$
    *   **Logic:**
        *   At $t=0$, $u = -\sin(\pi x)$ (Satisfies IC exactly).
        *   Since $\text{features}(x)$ are periodic and $-\sin(\pi x)$ is periodic on $[-1, 1]$, the total solution is Periodic (Satisfies BCs exactly).

### 5. Constraint Implementation Strategy
*   **Count:**
    *   $L_{pde}$ (1)
    *   $L_{ent}$ (1)
    *   $L_{ic}$ (Removed by Ansatz)
    *   $L_{bc}$ (Removed by Periodic Embeddings)
*   **Decision:** Use Hard Constraints (Ansatz) to reduce complexity.

### 6. Loss Function Assembly
*   **Symbolic Formula:**
    $$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{ent} $$
*   **Definitions:**
    *   $L_{pde} = \text{MSE}(u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2))$
    *   $L_{ent} = \text{Mean}(\text{ReLU}(\partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3))^2)$

### 6.1 Continuation Strategy
*   **None.** Solve the Inviscid equation directly using Entropy Regularization.

### 7. Weighting Strategy
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Total Terms:** 2 ($L_{pde}, L_{ent}$).
    *   **Strategy:** **Self-Balancing (GradNorm/RelLoss)**.
    *   **Instruction:** Update global weights dynamically during Adam. **FREEZE** them for SSBroyden.

### 8. Visualization Strategy
*   Standard 1D+t plots. Do not modify.

### 9. Saving Data
*   Save `u_pred` as `burgers_pred.npz` with keys `t`, `x`, `u`.

--- Strategy Report Received ---
### 0. Triage - Determine Strategy
*   **Strategy:** Path 2 (Advisor Recommendation) + Path 4 (Critic Correction).
*   **Combined Strategy:** Solve the **Viscous** Burgers' equation using **Continuation** on viscosity ($\nu$) down to the inviscid limit ($\nu \approx 10^{-4}$), while simultaneously enforcing **Entropy Regularization** to stabilize the shock.
*   **Formulation:** Use **Conservative Form** for the convective term.

### 1. Data Source
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.

### 2. Problem Statement
*   **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers' Equation (Conservative Convection).
    *   Residual: $r(u, \nu) = u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2) - \nu u_{xx}$
    *   **Note:** The parameter $\nu$ is dynamic (controlled by the training loop).
*   **Entropy Condition (Auxiliary Loss):**
    *   Entropy Pair: $\eta(u) = 0.5 u^2$, $q(u) = u^3/3$.
    *   Physical Condition: $\eta_t + q_x \le 0$ (Entropy must dissipate at shocks).
    *   Loss Term: $L_{ent} = \text{ReLU}(\partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3))^2$.
*   **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
*   **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
*   **IC:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
*   **Problem Type:** Forward.

### 2.1 Input
*   **Domain:** 1D space + time ($d=2$).

### 2.2 Output
*   **Value:** Scalar $u(t,x)$.
*   **Characteristics:** Shock formation expected.

### 3. Base Model Selection
*   **Type:** Low Dim (1D+t).
*   **Optimizer:** Hybrid Adam + SSBroyden.

### 4. Main Architecture
*   **Structure:** Input $\rightarrow$ Normalization $\rightarrow$ Embeddings $\rightarrow$ MLP $\rightarrow$ Output Transform $\rightarrow$ Output.

**4.1 Input Transformations**
*   **Normalization:** Normalize $x$ to $[-1, 1]$ and $t$ to $[0, 1]$.
*   **Feature Embeddings:**
    *   **Type:** **Periodic Fourier Features** (Option A) for $x$.
    *   **Configuration:** `degree = 2`.
    *   **Composition:** Pass **ONLY** the Fourier embeddings of $x$ and the raw normalized $t$ to the MLP.

**4.2 Main Body Architecture**
*   **Specs:** `width=30`, `depth=3`.
*   **Optimizer Strategy:**
    *   **Phase 1 (Adam):** 10,000 iterations. Static $\nu_{start}$. Dynamic Self-Balancing Weights.
    *   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** Continuation Schedule. **Frozen** Global Weights.

**4.3 Output Transformation (Hard Constraints)**
*   **Strategy:** Hard Constraints for IC and Periodic BCs.
*   **Ansatz:**
    $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, \text{features}(x)) $$
    *   **Logic:**
        *   At $t=0$, $u = -\sin(\pi x)$ (Satisfies IC).
        *   Since $\text{features}(x)$ and $-\sin(\pi x)$ are periodic on $[-1, 1]$, the solution is Periodic.

### 5. Constraint Implementation Strategy
*   **Count:**
    *   $L_{pde}$ (1)
    *   $L_{ent}$ (1)
    *   $L_{ic}$ (Removed by Ansatz)
    *   $L_{bc}$ (Removed by Periodic Embeddings)
*   **Decision:** Use Hard Constraints (Ansatz).

### 6. Loss Function Assembly
*   **Symbolic Formula:**
    $$ L_{total} = L_{pde}(\nu) + L_{ent} $$
*   **Definitions:**
    *   $L_{pde}(\nu) = \text{MSE}(u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2) - \nu u_{xx})$
    *   $L_{ent} = \text{Mean}(\text{ReLU}(\partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3))^2)$

### 6.1 Continuation Strategy (Mandatory)
*   **Parameter:** Viscosity $\nu$.
*   **Schedule:**
    1.  **Adam Phase:** $\nu = 0.01$ (Static, 10k iters).
    2.  **SSBroyden Phase (Continuation):**
        *   Step 1: $\nu = 0.005$ (Dwell: 5,000 iters)
        *   Step 2: $\nu = 0.002$ (Dwell: 10,000 iters)
        *   Step 3: $\nu = 0.001$ (Dwell: 15,000 iters)
        *   Step 4: $\nu = 0.0005$ (Dwell: 20,000 iters)
        *   Step 5: $\nu = 0.0001$ (Dwell: 25,000 iters - **Target**)
    *   **Instruction:** Reinitialize Hessian to Identity at each step.

### 7. Weighting Strategy
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Total Terms:** 2 ($L_{pde}, L_{ent}$).
    *   **Strategy:** **Self-Balancing**.
    *   **Instruction:** Update global weights dynamically during Adam. **FREEZE** them for SSBroyden.

### 8. Visualization Strategy
*   Standard 1D+t plots.

### 9. Saving Data
*   Save `u_pred` as `burgers_pred.npz` with keys `t`, `x`, `u`.

### Main Model
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]

### Enhancements
[Periodic Fourier Features]

--- Critic report---
[APPROVED]  
The plan respects all mandated constraints: viscous Burgers with ν-regularization plus entropy penalty in conservative form (Advisor), piecewise-constant ν continuation with ≥5 steps and increasing dwell times down to ν=1e‑4, SSBroyden Hessian reset each ν step, correct use of hard IC/periodic BC via ansatz (no boundary singularity), proper vRBA usage and freezing only global `lam` before SSBroyden, no forbidden variables or weak formulations, and data path/keys exactly match the user specification, with results saved separately as requested.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `Periodic_Fourier_Features` class to create a deterministic Fourier series expansion of the input coordinates. This is used to enforce periodic boundary conditions architecturally. This class is a standalone feature embedding layer and has no dependencies on other custom classes within the provided library.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

--- Analizing: # ## Cell 5: Model Architecture Definition (The PINN Structure) ---
  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
### [CURRENT_CELL_ID]
*   **Action:** Implement the hard constraint ansatz `u(t,x) = -sin(pi*x) + t * NN(t, x)` in the `PINN.__call__` method as required by the scientific strategy to enforce the initial condition.
*   **New Code (lines 35-43):**
    ```python
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, t: Array, x: Array) -> Array:
        # Normalize inputs
        t_norm = 2.0 * (t - self.lb[0]) / (self.ub[0] - self.lb[0]) - 1.0
        x_norm = 2.0 * (x - self.lb[1]) / (self.ub[1] - self.lb[1]) - 1.0
        
        x_embedded = self.enbedding(x_norm)
        X_concat = jnp.concatenate([t_norm, x_embedded], axis=-1)
        H = self.MLP(X_concat)
        
        # Apply hard constraint ansatz
        u = -jnp.sin(jnp.pi * x) + t * H
        return u
    ```

--- Processing: Cell 7 ---

--- Analizing: # ## Cell 7: PDE Residual Definition ---
  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
### [CURRENT_CELL_ID]
*   **Action:** Replace the non-conservative `Viscous_Burgers` residual with the conservative form `u_t + d/dx(0.5*u^2) - nu*uxx` as required by the scientific strategy for shock-capturing.
*   **Action:** Add the `Entropy_Residual` function `d/dt(0.5*u^2) + d/dx(u^3/3)` to implement the required entropy penalty for stabilizing the solution in the low-viscosity limit.
*   **New Code (lines 1-16):**
    ```python
    def Viscous_Burgers(params, t, x, nu, apply_fn):
        u = apply_fn(params, t, x)
        v_t = jnp.ones_like(t)
        v_x = jnp.ones_like(x)
        
        # First derivatives using jvp
        ut = jvp(lambda t_val: apply_fn(params, t_val, x), (t,), (v_t,))[1]
        
        # Conservative flux derivative: d/dx(0.5 * u^2)
        flux_fn = lambda u_val: 0.5 * u_val**2
        flux_x = jvp(lambda x_val: flux_fn(apply_fn(params, t, x_val)), (x,), (v_x,))[1]
        
        # Second derivative using hvp (Hessian-vector product)
        from Crunch.Models.layers import hvp_fwdfwd
        uxx = hvp_fwdfwd(lambda x_val: apply_fn(params, t, x_val), (x,), (v_x,))
        
        return ut + flux_x - nu * uxx

    def Entropy_Residual(params, t, x, apply_fn):
        v_t = jnp.ones_like(t)
        v_x = jnp.ones_like(x)

        # Define entropy and entropy flux functions
        eta_fn = lambda u_val: 0.5 * u_val**2
        q_fn = lambda u_val: u_val**3 / 3.0

        # d/dt(eta(u))
        eta_t = jvp(lambda t_val: eta_fn(apply_fn(params, t_val, x)), (t,), (v_t,))[1]

        # d/dx(q(u))
        q_x = jvp(lambda x_val: q_fn(apply_fn(params, t, x_val)), (x,), (v_x,))[1]

        return eta_t + q_x

    PDE_residuals=Viscous_Burgers
    ```

--- Analizing: # ## Cell 11: Main Training Step (Adam) & Gradient Calculation ---
  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
### [CURRENT_CELL_ID]
*   **Action:** Replace the initial condition loss (`loss_bcs`) with the entropy loss (`loss_entropy`) as mandated by the scientific strategy. This involves defining a new `loss_entropy` function that computes the ReLU of the entropy residual.
*   **Action:** Update the self-balancing logic to balance the PDE loss against the new entropy loss. This requires renaming variables like `lamB` to `lamEnt`, `gradient_bcs` to `gradient_ent`, and `grad_bar_BCs` to `grad_bar_Ent` throughout the function.
*   **Action:** Adjust the function signature to accept `lamEnt` instead of `lamB` and remove the unused `ti, xi, ui` from the unpacked `train_data`.
*   **New Code (lines 1-47):**
    ```python
    @partial(jax.jit, static_argnums=(0, 1))
    def apply_model(
        apply_fn,
        update_weights_fn,
        params, lambdas, lamEnt,
        all_grads,  #
        idx_train, it,
        hyperparams, nu,
        *train_data
    ):
        lamE,gamma, eta, gamma_grads, alpha = hyperparams
        t, x, _, _, _ = train_data # ti, xi, ui are not used due to ansatz
        lambdas_it = lambdas[idx_train]
        def loss_pde(params, lambdas):
            residuals = PDE_residuals(params, t, x, nu, apply_fn)
            r_i_norm = lax.stop_gradient(jnp.abs(residuals))
            new_lambdas = update_weights_fn(r_i_norm,lambdas, it,
                                              gamma=gamma,
                                              eta=eta)
            pde_loss = jnp.mean((new_lambdas * residuals) ** 2)
            return pde_loss, new_lambdas
        
        def loss_entropy(params):
            ent_residuals = Entropy_Residual(params, t, x, apply_fn)
            # Loss is ReLU of the residual, squared, as per strategy
            entropy_loss = jnp.mean(nn.relu(ent_residuals)**2)
            return entropy_loss

        (pde_loss, new_lambdas), gradient_pde = jax.value_and_grad(loss_pde, has_aux=True)(params, lambdas_it)
        entropy_loss, gradient_ent = jax.value_and_grad(loss_entropy)(params)
        lambdas = lambdas.at[idx_train].set(new_lambdas)
        
        pde_gradient_flat, _ = ravel_pytree(gradient_pde)
        ent_gradient_flat, _ = ravel_pytree(gradient_ent)
        
        grad_PDE_norm = jnp.linalg.norm(pde_gradient_flat) + 1e-20
        grad_ent_norm = jnp.linalg.norm(ent_gradient_flat) + 1e-20
        
        # Update moving averages of gradient norms
        grad_avg_PDE = gamma_grads * all_grads['grad_bar_PDE'] + (1.0 - gamma_grads) * grad_PDE_norm
        grad_avg_Ent = gamma_grads * all_grads['grad_bar_Ent'] + (1.0 - gamma_grads) * grad_ent_norm
        
        # Update self-balancing weight for entropy loss
        lamEnt = alpha * lamEnt + (1.0 - alpha) * grad_avg_PDE / (grad_avg_Ent + 1e-20)
        
        # Combine gradients
        total_gradient = jax.tree_util.tree_map(lambda g_pde, g_ent: lamE * g_pde + lamEnt * g_ent, gradient_pde, gradient_ent)
        
        all_loss = {
            'loss_PDE': pde_loss,
            'loss_Ent': entropy_loss,
            'Loss': lamE * pde_loss + lamEnt * entropy_loss,
        }
        all_grads_updated = {
            'grad_bar_PDE': grad_avg_PDE,
            'grad_bar_Ent': grad_avg_Ent,
        }
        return all_loss, total_gradient, lambdas, lamEnt, all_grads_updated
    ```

--- Analizing: # ## Cell 16: Defining the Loss Functions for Second-Order Optimization ---
  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
### [CURRENT_CELL_ID]
*   **Action:** Modify `apply_model_2nd_Order` to incorporate the entropy loss, replacing the now-obsolete initial condition loss. The total loss will be `loss_pde + lamEnt * loss_entropy`, using the frozen `lamEnt` from the Adam phase.
*   **Action:** Update the function signature of `apply_model_2nd_Order` to accept `lamEnt` instead of `lamB` and to correctly unpack the training data, ignoring the unused IC/BC points.

*   **New Code (lines 1-17):**
    ```python
    @partial(jax.jit, static_argnums=(0,))
    def apply_model_2nd_Order(apply_fn, params, nu, lamEnt, *train_data):
        t, x, _, _, _ = train_data # ti, xi, ui are not used
        def loss_pde(params):
            # Compute residuals
            residuals = PDE_residuals(params, t, x, nu, apply_fn)
            pde_loss = jnp.mean((residuals)**2)
            return pde_loss
        def loss_entropy(params):
            # Compute entropy loss
            ent_residuals = Entropy_Residual(params, t, x, apply_fn)
            entropy_loss = jnp.mean(nn.relu(ent_residuals)**2)
            return entropy_loss
        def loss(params):
            return loss_pde(params) + lamEnt * loss_entropy(params)
        loss_value=loss(params)
        return loss_value
    ```
*   **New Code (lines 31-37):**
    ```python
    @partial(jax.jit, static_argnums=(1, 2))
    def loss_and_gradient(weights, N_arg, unflatten_func_arg, *train_data_tuple_arg):
        flat_jax_array = weights
        params_current = unflatten_func_arg(flat_jax_array)
        *actual_train_data, nu, lamEnt = train_data_tuple_arg
        loss_val_jax = apply_model_2nd_Order(N_arg, params_current, nu, lamEnt, *actual_train_data)
        return loss_val_jax
    ```

--- Analizing: # ## Cell 5: Model Architecture Definition (The PINN Structure) ---
  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
### [CURRENT_CELL_ID]
*   **Action:** Correct the `IndentationError` identified in the `[ERROR_REPORT]`. The `@nn.compact` decorator and the `__call__` method for the `PINN` class must be indented to be part of the class definition.
*   **New Code (lines 24-46):**
    ```python
    class PINN(nn.Module):
        features: Sequence[int]
        lb: jnp.ndarray
        ub: jnp.ndarray
        embedding_degree: int = 1
        Lx: float = 2.0
        activation: Callable = nn.tanh
        def setup(self):
             self.enbedding = Periodic_Fourier_Features(kmax=self.embedding_degree, Lx=self.Lx)
             self.MLP = MLP(features=self.features, activation=self.activation)
    
        @nn.compact
        def __call__(self, t: Array, x: Array) -> Array:
            # Normalize inputs
            t_norm = 2.0 * (t - self.lb[0]) / (self.ub[0] - self.lb[0]) - 1.0
            x_norm = 2.0 * (x - self.lb[1]) / (self.ub[1] - self.lb[1]) - 1.0
    
            x_embedded = self.enbedding(x_norm)
            X_concat = jnp.concatenate([t_norm, x_embedded], axis=-1)
            H = self.MLP(X_concat)
    
            # Apply hard constraint ansatz
            u = -jnp.sin(jnp.pi * x) + t * H
            return u
    ```

**Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback**
*   **Observation:**
    *   **Loss & Error History:** The training process shows distinct phases. The initial Adam phase (0-10k iterations) demonstrates a steady decrease in both loss and error. Upon switching to SSBroyden at 10k iterations, the L2 error drops sharply and converges to a stable plateau around 0.135. However, the total loss becomes extremely unstable and oscillatory, fluctuating by orders of magnitude (between 1e-3 and 1e-1) for the remainder of the training. This indicates optimizer instability, even though the solution error has converged.
    *   **Solution & Error Fields:** The predicted solution field is visually an excellent match to the reference, correctly capturing the wave steepening and shock formation. The pointwise error plot confirms this qualitative assessment: the error is negligible in the smooth regions of the flow but is highly concentrated along the shock front, where it reaches magnitudes of ~10^-1. The PDE residual plot shows a similar pattern, with the largest residuals located at the shock.

*   **Diagnosis:** **Stagnation due to Spectral Bias.** The model has converged to a stable but inaccurate solution (final L2 error of 13.5%). The error is almost entirely localized at the shock front. This is a classic case of spectral bias, where the network's basis functions are unable to represent the high-frequency, discontinuous nature of the shock. The chosen Periodic Fourier Features with `degree=2` provide only low-frequency modes, making it impossible for the model to capture the sharp gradient accurately. The severe oscillations in the loss plot during the SSBroyden phase are a secondary symptom of the problem's stiffness, which the optimizer struggles with when the model lacks the capacity to represent the true solution.

*   **Targeted Improvements:**
    *   **For Integrity:** None
    *   **For Accuracy:** The primary bottleneck is the model's limited frequency representation. The current Periodic Fourier Features with `degree=2` are insufficient to capture the shock. **Increase the embedding degree significantly, for example, to `degree=16`**, to provide the model with the necessary high-frequency basis functions to resolve the sharp gradient.
    *   **For Details:** The loss history during the SSBroyden phase is highly oscillatory. While the L2 error is stable, this loss instability suggests the optimizer is struggling. Improving the model's capacity to represent the solution (as recommended for Accuracy) should also help stabilize the optimization landscape.
    *   **For Optimality:**
        *   *Constraint Formulation:* None. The use of conservative form is correct.
        *   *Output Transforms:* None. The ansatz correctly enforces the IC.
        *   *Entropy (FOR SHOCKS - CRITICAL):* The current method correctly uses an entropy penalty and conservative form, which is excellent and essential for this problem. No changes needed.

--- Strategy Report Received ---
### 0. Triage - Determine Strategy
*   **Strategy:** Path 2 (Advisor Recommendation).
*   **Advisor Mandate:** The Advisor explicitly attributes failure to **Spectral Bias** and mandates increasing the Periodic Fourier Feature embedding to **`degree=16`**.
*   **Data Role:** Condition B (Validation Data).

### 1. Data Source
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.
*   **Instruction:** Load this data for validation and error metric computation ONLY.

### 2. Problem Statement
*   **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers' Equation (Conservative Form, Vanishing Viscosity approximation for Inviscid limit):
    $$ R = u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2) - \nu \partial_{xx} u $$
    *   **Note:** We introduce a learnable parameter $\nu$ to stabilize shock formation, annealing it towards 0.
*   **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
*   **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
*   **ICs:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
*   **Problem Type:** Forward.
*   **Well-Posedness:** The inviscid problem develops shocks. We use the vanishing viscosity method ($\nu \to 0$) combined with an entropy constraint to select the physical weak solution.

### 2.1 Input
*   **Domain:** 1D space ($x$) + time ($t$).

### 2.2 Output
*   **Value:** Scalar $u(t,x)$.
*   **Characteristics:** Shock formation (discontinuity) at $x=0$.

### 3. Base Model Selection
*   **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.
*   **Reasoning:** 1D+t domain requires the standard robust optimizer pair.

### 4. Main Architecture
*   **Structure:** Input $\rightarrow$ Normalization $\rightarrow$ Embedding $\rightarrow$ Main Body $\rightarrow$ Output Transformation $\rightarrow$ Output.

**4.1 Input Transformations**
*   **Normalization:**
    *   Normalize $x$ to $[-1, 1]$.
    *   Normalize $t$ to $[0, 1]$.
    *   **Constraint:** Apply normalization *inside* the model's call function.
*   **Feature Embeddings:**
    *   **Selection:** **Periodic Fourier Features** (Option A).
    *   **Configuration:** Apply to $x$ **ONLY**. Pass raw normalized $t$.
    *   **Degree:** **`degree=16`** (Strict Advisor Mandate to fix Spectral Bias).
    *   **Composition:** Concatenate [Embedded $x$, Normalized $t$].

**4.2 Main Body Architecture**
*   **Specs:** Case A (1D+t).
    *   **Width:** 30.
    *   **Depth:** 3.
    *   **Activation:** Tanh.

**4.3 Output Transformation (Hard Constraints)**
*   **Ansatz:** $u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t,x)$.
    *   **Logic:**
        *   At $t=0$, $u = -\sin(\pi x)$ (Exact IC).
        *   Periodicity is enforced strictly by the Periodic Fourier Features on $x$.
        *   Therefore, no soft loss terms are needed for IC or BCs.

### 5. Constraint Implementation Strategy
*   **Strategy:** **Architectural (Hard Constraints)**.
*   **Count:** Only $L_{pde}$ and $L_{entropy}$ are required in the loss. IC and BCs are satisfied by construction.

### 6. Loss Function Assembly
*   **Symbolic Formula:**
    $$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{entropy} $$
*   **Components:**
    *   **$L_{pde}$:** MSE of residual $u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2) - \nu u_{xx}$.
    *   **$L_{entropy}$:** Entropy condition to ensure physical shock selection.
        $$ L_{entropy} = \text{Mean}(\text{ReLU}( \partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) )^2) $$
        (Enforces $\eta_t + q_x \le 0$ where $\eta=u^2/2, q=u^3/3$).

### 6.1 Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)
*   **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
*   **Phase 1 (Adam):**
    *   **State:** Static $\nu = 0.01$.
    *   **Iterations:** 10,000.
*   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):**
    *   **State:** Dynamic Annealing.
    *   **Schedule:** $\nu = [0.005, 0.002, 0.001, 0.0005, 0.0001]$.
    *   **Dwell Times:** Increase dwell with difficulty (e.g., 5k, 10k, 15k, 20k, 25k iterations).
    *   **Instruction:** Reinitialize Hessian at each $\nu$ step.

### 7. Weighting Strategy
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Terms:** 2 ($L_{pde}, L_{entropy}$).
    *   **Strategy:** **Self-Balancing**.
    *   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamic updates (GradNorm/RelLoss).
    *   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** global weights at final Adam values.

### 8. Visualization Strategy
*   Standard 1D+t plots. Do not modify template visualization.

### 9. Saving Data
*   Save `u_pred` and `u_exact` (interpolated from data) to `.npz`.

**Main Model:**
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]

**Enhancements:**
[Periodic Fourier Features]

--- Critic report---
[REJECTED]

**Critique:** Violates the Reynolds/viscosity continuation bounds and zero-viscosity trap: the target problem is inviscid (effectively Re → ∞), but the plan anneals viscosity only down to ν = 1e-4 instead of to a small positive value as a surrogate for the inviscid limit with explicit acknowledgment of that target, and it describes “ν → 0” without enforcing a strict lower bound >0 per the stability mandate. Also, the continuation ladder rule requires the *final* target to correspond to Re ≳ 1e4 (ν ≲ 1e-4); the current schedule stops at 1e-4 without clarifying that this is the final target physics and not a further vanishing step, creating an inconsistency with the mandated ν-annealing scheme for shock problems.

**Requirement:**  
Clarify and correct the vanishing-viscosity strategy to satisfy the shock/zero-viscosity rules and Reynolds ladder:  
1. Explicitly set a **small but nonzero final ν**, e.g. ν_final = 1e-4 (or slightly smaller like 5e-5) and state that this is the **fixed surrogate** for the inviscid solution; do **not** claim ν → 0 or true ν=0 in the training PDE.  
2. Ensure the continuation schedule is consistent and clearly targets that ν_final as the **last step** (e.g. ν = [1e-2, 5e-3, 2e-3, 1e-3, 5e-4, 1e-4] with monotonically increasing dwell times, final step ≥25k iters). Confirm no further annealing beyond this ν_final.  
3. State explicitly that both **viscosity regularization** (the fixed ν_final) and the **entropy penalty** are used together, and that the strong-form PDE is never run at ν=0.

### 2.1 Input

### 2.2 Output
*   **Value:** Scalar $u(t,x)$.
*   **Characteristics:** Shock formation at $x=0$.

### 3. Base Model Selection
*   **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.

### 4. Main Architecture
*   **Structure:** Input $\rightarrow$ Normalization $\rightarrow$ Embedding $\rightarrow$ Main Body $\rightarrow$ Output Transformation $\rightarrow$ Output.

**4.1 Input Transformations**
*   **Normalization:**
    *   Normalize $x$ to $[-1, 1]$.
    *   Normalize $t$ to $[0, 1]$.
    *   **Constraint:** Apply normalization *inside* the model's call function.
*   **Feature Embeddings:**
    *   **Selection:** **Periodic Fourier Features** (Option A).
    *   **Configuration:** Apply to $x$ **ONLY**. Pass raw normalized $t$.
    *   **Degree:** **`degree=16`** (Strict Advisor Mandate).
    *   **Composition:** Concatenate [Embedded $x$, Normalized $t$].

**4.2 Main Body Architecture**
*   **Specs:** Case A (1D+t).
    *   **Width:** 30.
    *   **Depth:** 3.
    *   **Activation:** Tanh.

**4.3 Output Transformation (Hard Constraints)**
*   **Ansatz:** $u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t,x)$.
    *   **Logic:**
        *   At $t=0$, $u = -\sin(\pi x)$ (Exact IC).
        *   Periodicity is enforced strictly by the Periodic Fourier Features on $x$ (both the NN and the sine term are periodic).
        *   Therefore, no soft loss terms are needed for IC or BCs.

### 5. Constraint Implementation Strategy
*   **Strategy:** **Architectural (Hard Constraints)**.
*   **Count:** Only $L_{pde}$ and $L_{entropy}$ are required in the loss.

### 6. Loss Function Assembly
*   **Symbolic Formula:**
    $$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{entropy} $$
*   **Components:**
    *   **$L_{pde}$:** MSE of residual $u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2) - \nu u_{xx}$.
    *   **$L_{entropy}$:** Entropy condition to ensure physical shock selection.
        $$ L_{entropy} = \text{Mean}(\text{ReLU}( \partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) )^2) $$

### 6.1 Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)
*   **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
*   **Phase 1 (Adam):**
    *   **State:** Static $\nu = 0.01$.
    *   **Iterations:** 10,000.
*   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):**
    *   **State:** Dynamic Annealing.
    *   **Schedule:** $\nu = [0.005, 0.002, 0.001, 0.0005, 0.0001]$.
    *   **Dwell Times:** Increase dwell with difficulty (e.g., 5k, 10k, 10k, 15k, 25k iterations).
    *   **Instruction:** Reinitialize Hessian at each $\nu$ step.
    *   **Target:** The final training happens at $\nu = 10^{-4}$. Do not go lower.

### 7. Weighting Strategy
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Terms:** 2 ($L_{pde}, L_{entropy}$).
    *   **Strategy:** **Self-Balancing**.
    *   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamic updates (GradNorm/RelLoss).
    *   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** global weights at final Adam values.

### 8. Visualization Strategy
*   Standard 1D+t plots. Do not modify template visualization.

### 9. Saving Data
*   Save `u_pred` and `u_exact` (interpolated from data) to `.npz`.

**Main Model:**
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]

**Enhancements:**
[Periodic Fourier Features]

--- Critic report---
[APPROVED]

The plan matches all required constraints:  
- **Physics & Regularization:** Uses viscous Burgers in conservative form with entropy penalty and a **fixed lower bound ν_final = 1e-4** (no ν=0), satisfying the zero-viscosity/shock rules and Reynolds ladder (target Re ~ 1e4).  
- **Continuation:** Viscosity continuation is **stepwise**, with ν piecewise constant per stage, **Hessian reinitialized** at each ν, and **increasing dwell times**, with the hardest (ν=1e-4) step ≥ 25k iterations.  
- **Advisor Compliance & Spectral Bias:** Fully follows Advisor’s directive by raising **Fourier feature degree to 16**; keeps the successful conservative form, entropy loss, and IC ansatz.  
- **Data & Problem Definition:** Uses the **exact user path and keys** only for validation; all symbols (ν, domain, IC, BCs) are numerically and conceptually specified; solves the requested inviscid problem via a clearly stated viscous surrogate.  
- **Optimization & Weights:** Hybrid Adam→SSBroyden with **global weights frozen** before SSBroyden, vRBA lambdas used for weighting/sampling in Adam and sampling-only in SSBroyden, preserving Hessian integrity.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `Periodic_Fourier_Features` class to create a deterministic Fourier series expansion of the input coordinates. This is used to enforce periodic boundary conditions architecturally. This class is a standalone feature embedding layer and has no dependencies on other custom classes within the provided library.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

The comment `# Normalize inputs` at line 223 is **not** a valid indented block. Python requires at least one indented statement after a function definition. Line 224 (`t_norm = ...`) has **zero indentation**, causing it to be interpreted as outside the function body.

**Bug Analysis for Planner:**
1. **The Problem:** Line 224 is not indented relative to the function definition at line 222. Python syntax requires all statements inside a function to be indented by at least one level (typically 4 spaces or 1 tab).

2. **The Fix:** Line 224 (and all subsequent lines in the `__call__` method through line 230) must be indented by 4 spaces (or 1 tab) to be inside the function body.

3. **Correct Structure:**
```python
@nn.compact
def __call__(self, t: Array, x: Array) -> Array:
    # Normalize inputs
    t_norm = (t - self.lb[0]) / (self.ub[0] - self.lb[0])  # Must be indented
    x_norm = 2.0 * (x - self.lb[1]) / (self.ub[1] - self.lb[1]) - 1.0
    # ... rest of method body
```

    ```python
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, t: Array, x: Array) -> Array:
        # Normalize inputs
        t_norm = (t - self.lb[0]) / (self.ub[0] - self.lb[0])
        x_norm = 2.0 * (x - self.lb[1]) / (self.ub[1] - self.lb[1]) - 1.0
        x_embedded = self.enbedding(x_norm)
        X_concat = jnp.concatenate([t_norm, x_embedded], axis=-1)
        H = self.MLP(X_concat)
        # Apply hard constraint ansatz
        u = -jnp.sin(jnp.pi * x) + t * H
        return u
    ```

- **LINE_222:** Correctly closes the `setup(self)` method with the `MLP` initialization.
- **LINE_223:** Adds `@nn.compact` decorator (correct placement for the `__call__` method).

This creates an invalid syntax structure where Python expects an indented block after the first `@nn.compact` (LINE_223), but instead encounters another decorator at the same indentation level.

**The Fix:**

```python
# LINE_220: def setup(self):
# LINE_221:     self.enbedding = Periodic_Fourier_Features(...)
# LINE_222:     self.MLP = MLP(...)
# LINE_223: @nn.compact
# LINE_224: def __call__(self, t: Array, x: Array) -> Array:  # <-- Remove duplicate decorator here
```

2. Verify that the `__call__` method (LINE_225) is properly indented under the class and preceded by only ONE `@nn.compact` decorator (LINE_223).

--- Analizing: # ## Cell 5: Model Architecture Definition (The PINN Structure) ---
  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
### [CURRENT_CELL_ID] 5
*   **Action:** The `[ERROR_REPORT]` identified a fatal `IndentationError` caused by a duplicate `@nn.compact` decorator on line 35. This line will be removed to fix the syntax error. The scientific logic of the cell already matches the `[SCIENTIFIC_STRATEGY_REPORT]`.
*   **New Code (deleting line 35):**
    ```python
    class PINN(nn.Module):
        features: Sequence[int]
        lb: jnp.ndarray
        ub: jnp.ndarray
        embedding_degree: int = 1
        Lx: float = 2.0
        activation: Callable = nn.tanh
        def setup(self):
            self.enbedding = Periodic_Fourier_Features(kmax=self.embedding_degree, Lx=self.Lx)
            self.MLP = MLP(features=self.features, activation=self.activation)
        @nn.compact
        def __call__(self, t: Array, x: Array) -> Array:
            # Normalize inputs
            t_norm = (t - self.lb[0]) / (self.ub[0] - self.lb[0])
            x_norm = 2.0 * (x - self.lb[1]) / (self.ub[1] - self.lb[1]) - 1.0
            x_embedded = self.enbedding(x_norm)
            X_concat = jnp.concatenate([t_norm, x_embedded], axis=-1)
            H = self.MLP(X_concat)
            # Apply hard constraint ansatz
            u = -jnp.sin(jnp.pi * x) + t * H
            return u
    ```

REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT

**Section 1: Status Decision**
*   **Status:** `REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT`

**Section 2: Quantitative Scorecard**
*   **Score Integrity:** 35
*   **Score Accuracy:** 35
*   **Score Details:** 7
*   **Score Optimality:** 15
*   **TOTAL SCORE:** 92

**DECISION LOGIC:**
The total score is high, indicating a very successful run in terms of accuracy. However, the run is rejected because the loss and error curves in the final training stage (after the last continuation step) are highly oscillatory and do not converge to a stable plateau. A stable plateau is a mandatory requirement for an approved run, as it signifies that the optimizer has found a stable minimum.

**Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback**
*   **Observation:**
    1.  **Loss & Error History:** The training history clearly shows the multi-stage continuation strategy. The Adam pre-training phase is followed by several SSBroyden stages where viscosity is lowered, causing sharp drops in both loss and error. The final stage, beginning around iteration 37,500, successfully reduces the error to an excellent minimum of ~1.27e-3. However, this final segment is characterized by severe, high-frequency oscillations in both the loss (fluctuating between 1e-4 and 1e-6) and the relative L2 error (fluctuating between ~2e-3 and 1e-2). The training does not settle into a stable plateau.
    2.  **Residual/Pointwise Error:** The pointwise error and PDE residual plots correctly identify the shock front at x=0 as the primary source of error and difficulty. This is physically expected.
    3.  **Solution Fields:** Visually, the predicted solution is nearly indistinguishable from the reference solution. The model has successfully captured the shock formation and overall dynamics of the Burgers' equation.

*   **Diagnosis:** **Stiffness-Induced Oscillations in Final Stage.**
    The model achieves excellent accuracy, but the optimization process becomes unstable during the final, most challenging stage (at the lowest viscosity $\nu=10^{-4}$). The combination of a stiff PDE, a sensitive second-order optimizer (SSBroyden), and very high-degree Fourier features (`degree=16`) has created a noisy and difficult loss landscape. The optimizer finds the correct region for the solution but cannot settle into a stable minimum, resulting in the observed oscillations.

*   **Targeted Improvements:**
    *   **For Integrity:** None.
    *   **For Accuracy:** The primary issue is not accuracy but stability. To stabilize the final convergence phase, the model's capacity for high-frequency noise should be slightly tempered.
        *   **Recommendation:** **Reduce the Fourier Feature embedding degree from `degree=16` to `degree=12`.** This will help smooth the loss landscape, reducing the high-frequency modes that are likely causing the oscillations, and should allow the SSBroyden optimizer to converge more stably.
    *   **For Details:** The main goal of the next run is to eliminate the final-stage oscillations. The loss and error curves must exhibit a stable, flat plateau after the last continuation step.
    *   **For Optimality:** None. The methodological choices (vRBA, Entropy Penalty, Conservative Form, Hybrid Optimizer) are state-of-the-art and appropriate for this problem.

--- 🔄 Strategy Iteration 1/5 ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
### 0. Triage - Determine Strategy
*   **User Request:** Solve 1D Inviscid Burgers' equation with Periodic BCs.
*   **Advisor Report:** **Mandatory Refinement.** The previous run (Score 92) was accurate but unstable in the final stage.
*   **Advisor Recommendation:** **Reduce Fourier Feature embedding degree from 16 to 12** to eliminate high-frequency oscillations and ensure a stable plateau.
*   **Strategy:** Follow the "Vanishing Viscosity" approach (annealing $\nu \to 0$) used in the successful Trial 10, but apply the Advisor's architectural constraint.

### 1. Data Source
*   **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.

### 2. Problem Statement
*   **Governing Equation (Vanishing Viscosity Form):**
    To solve the Inviscid Burgers' equation with shocks, we use the viscous form and anneal $\nu$ to a small value.
    $$ r_{pde} = u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx} $$
    *   **Entropy Condition:** To ensure the correct physical weak solution (shock), we enforce the entropy inequality:
        $$ r_{ent} = \partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) \le 0 $$
*   **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
*   **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
*   **IC:** $u(x, 0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
*   **Problem Type:** Forward (Well-posed via Vanishing Viscosity).
*   **Data Loss:** None.

### 2.1 Input
*   **Dimensions:** $d=2$ ($t, x$).

### 2.2 Output
*   **Dimensions:** $q=1$ ($u$).
*   **Characteristics:** Shock formation at $x=0$.

### 3. Base Model Selection
*   **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.
*   **Reasoning:** 1D+t problem requires the precision of SSBroyden for the shock, initialized by Adam.

### 4. Main Architecture
**Structure:** `Input -> Input Transformation -> Main Body -> Output Transformation -> Output`

**4.1 Input Transformations**
*   **Normalization:**
    *   Normalize $t$ to $[0, 1]$.
    *   Normalize $x$ to $[-1, 1]$.
    *   **CRITICAL:** Apply normalization *inside* the model's `forward` method.
*   **Feature Embeddings (Advisor Mandate):**
    *   **Type:** **Periodic Fourier Features**.
    *   **Input:** Apply *only* to normalized $x$. Pass normalized $t$ raw.
    *   **Configuration:** **`degree=12`** (Reduced from 16 as per Advisor). `scale=1`.
    *   **Logic:** Enforces Periodic BCs architecturally while reducing high-frequency noise.

**4.2 Main Body Architecture**
*   **Specs:** `width=30`, `depth=3` (Standard Case A).
*   **Activation:** `tanh`.

**4.3 Output Transformation (Hard Constraints)**
*   **Ansatz:** Enforce the Initial Condition ($u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$) exactly.
    $$ u_{pred}(t, x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, x_{embed}) $$
*   **Note:** Periodic BCs are satisfied by the Input Embedding. IC is satisfied by this Ansatz.

### 5. Constraint Implementation Strategy
*   **Strategy:** **Architectural (Hard Constraints)**.
    *   BCs: Periodic Fourier Features.
    *   IC: Time-factor Ansatz.
*   **Loss Terms Required:** `L_pde`, `L_entropy`. (No BC/IC loss terms needed).

### 6. Loss Function Assembly
$$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{entropy} $$

*   **Definitions:**
    *   `L_pde`: MSE of $r_{pde}$ (using current $\nu$).
    *   `L_entropy`: MSE of $\text{ReLU}(r_{ent})$. (Penalize positive entropy production).

### 6.1 Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)
*   **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
*   **Phase 1 (Adam):**
    *   $\nu = 0.01$ (Static).
    *   Iterations: 10,000.
*   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):**
    *   **Schedule:** $\nu \in [0.005, 0.002, 0.001, 0.0005, 0.0001]$.
    *   **Dwell Times:**
        *   $\nu=0.005$: 5,000 iters.
        *   $\nu=0.002$: 10,000 iters.
        *   $\nu=0.001$: 10,000 iters.
        *   $\nu=0.0005$: 15,000 iters.
        *   $\nu=0.0001$: 25,000 iters.
    *   **Instruction:** Re-initialize Hessian to Identity at each $\nu$ step.

### 7. Weighting Strategy
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for `L_pde`.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Strategy:** **Self-Balancing**.
    *   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamic updates (GradNorm/RelLoss).
    *   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** global weights at final Adam values.

### 8. Visualization Strategy
*   Standard plots. Ensure `u` is plotted.

### 9. Saving Data
*   Save `u_pred` as `.npz` with keys `t`, `x`, `u_pred`.

### List of Specialized Modules
Main Model:
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]

Enhancements:
[Periodic Fourier Features]

--- Critic report---
[REJECTED]

**Critique:** Violates the Continuation Rules: the SSBroyden viscosity schedule reuses an equal dwell time for two consecutive steps (ν=0.002 and ν=0.001 both at 10,000 iterations), so iteration counts do not increase monotonically across all continuation steps, as required.

**Requirement:** Adjust the continuation schedule so that *every* successive viscosity step has a strictly non-decreasing (preferably increasing) iteration count, with at least 5 steps and the final ν stage having ≥25,000 iterations (which you already satisfy). For example: [0.005: 5k, 0.002: 10k, 0.001: 15k, 0.0005: 20k, 0.0001: 25k].

- **Physics & Regularization:** Inviscid Burgers with shocks is handled via viscous Burgers + vanishing viscosity (ν down to 1e-4) and an entropy penalty; no ν=0 strong form, so the zero-viscosity trap is avoided.  
- **Continuation Strategy:** Uses discrete, piecewise-constant ν steps (0.01 → 0.005 → 0.002 → 0.001 → 0.0005 → 0.0001), monotonically increasing dwell times (5k,10k,15k,20k,25k) with ≥5 steps and final step ≥25k, and explicitly reinitializes the SSBroyden Hessian at each ν step.  
- **Optimization & Weights:** Hybrid Adam → SSBroyden, with self-balancing global weights during Adam and global weights frozen for SSBroyden; vRBA `lambdas` used for weighting/sampling in Adam and sampling in SSBroyden, not frozen.  
- **Boundary/Initial Conditions:** Periodic BCs handled via periodic Fourier features on x, IC enforced via hard ansatz u(t,x)=−sin(πx)+t·NN(...); no conflicting singular Dirichlet corners.  
- **Advisor Compliance:** Advisor’s directive to reduce Fourier feature degree from 16 to 12 is followed; no weak formulation is introduced.  
- **Data Integrity:** Uses the exact user path and keys (`/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`, keys `t`, `x`, `usol`) for error metrics only, and instructs saving predictions as `.npz` with `t`, `x`, `u_pred`.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires the `Periodic_Fourier_Features` class to create a deterministic Fourier series expansion of the input coordinates. This is used to enforce periodic boundary conditions architecturally. This class is a standalone feature embedding layer and has no dependencies on other custom classes within the provided library.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

The initial Adam phase successfully reduces the error, and each subsequent SSBroyden stage (with decreasing viscosity) achieves a lower error floor. However, the final stage, starting around 40,000 iterations, is highly unstable. Both the total loss and the relative L2 error exhibit severe, high-frequency oscillations spanning multiple orders of magnitude (loss oscillates between ~$10^{-6}$ and $10^{-3}$; error between ~$10^{-3}$ and $10^{-2}$). *   **Diagnosis:** **High-frequency oscillations in the final segment.** The previous run (Trial 10, Score 92) was flagged for this same issue. The corrective action—reducing the Fourier feature degree from 16 to 12—was insufficient to stabilize the training. The problem's stiffness, originating from the sharp shock at very low viscosity, creates a chaotic optimization landscape that the standard MLP architecture coupled with the SSBroyden optimizer cannot navigate stably. The model has sufficient capacity to find an accurate solution but lacks the architectural stability to remain there.

*   **Targeted Improvements:**
    *   **For Integrity:** None
    *   **For Accuracy:** The model is struggling with stiffness from the shock, causing severe oscillations in the final training stage. Switch the main model from MLP to a **Kolmogorov-Arnold Network (KAN)** to better capture the stiff dynamics. **Crucially, maintain the Periodic Fourier Feature embedding as the input to the KAN** to preserve the architectural enforcement of periodic boundary conditions. A suitable univariate basis for the KAN would be **Chebyshev polynomials**.
    *   **For Details:** The primary issue is the lack of a stable plateau in the final loss/error segment, which is addressed by the accuracy recommendation. No separate action needed.
    *   **For Optimality:**
        *   *Constraint Formulation:* For 2D NS, use Stream Function ($\psi$). For 3D NS, use Vector Potential ($\vec{A}$). This reduces unknowns and enforces $\nabla \cdot u = 0$ exactly.
        *   *Output Transforms:* Enforce positivity (e.g., Density/Pressure) via Output Transform: $u = \text{Softplus}(\text{Net}(x)) + \epsilon$.
        *   *Entropy (FOR SHOCKS - CRITICAL):* For inviscid/low-viscosity problems with shocks (Burgers $\nu \le 1e-3$, Euler), impose Entropy Penalty $(\eta, q)$ and use conservative form.
