=== 🧠 Starting Strategy Team (Proposer-Critic Loop) ===

--- 🔄 Strategy Iteration 1/5 ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
### Analysis Steps

**0. Triage - Determine Strategy**
- **User Request:** Solve 1D Inviscid Burgers' equation with Periodic BCs and specific IC.
- **Advisor Report:**
    - **Diagnosis:** Previous MLP models achieved high accuracy but suffered from high-frequency oscillations (stiffness) in the final vanishing viscosity stages.
    - **Recommendation:** Switch Main Body to **Kolmogorov-Arnold Network (KAN)** using **Chebyshev polynomials**.
    - **Constraint:** **MUST** maintain **Periodic Fourier Features** on input $x$ to enforce periodicity.
    - **Optimality:** Use Entropy Penalty for shocks.
- **Strategy:** Follow Advisor's architecture (Fourier -> KAN) and the successful Vanishing Viscosity continuation strategy from previous trials.

**1. Data Source**
- **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.
- **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
- **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.

**2. Problem Statement**
- **Governing Equation (Viscous Form for Continuation):**
  $$ u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx} = 0 $$
  (where $\nu$ is a learnable/annealed parameter).
- **Initial Condition:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
- **Boundary Conditions:** Periodic in $x$.
- **Entropy Condition (Inviscid Limit):**
  $$ \partial_t(u^2/2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) \le 0 $$
- **Problem Type:** Forward.
- **Well-Posedness:** Well-posed via Vanishing Viscosity method.

**2.1. Input**
- Domain: $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.

**2.2. Output**
- $u(t,x)$ (Scalar).
- Characteristics: Shock formation at $t \approx 1/\pi$. High gradients.

**3. Base Model Selection**
- **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden. (Base template, modified to KAN).

**4. Main Architecture**
- **Structure:** Input $\to$ Normalization $\to$ Embeddings $\to$ **KAN Main Body** $\to$ Output Transform $\to$ Output.

**4.1. Input Transformations**
- **Normalization:**
    - $x_{norm} = x$ (already in $[-1, 1]$).
    - $t_{norm} = t$ (already in $[0, 1]$).
    - **CRITICAL:** Apply normalization *inside* the model's `forward` method.
- **Feature Embeddings:**
    - **Periodic Fourier Features:** Applied to $x_{norm}$ **ONLY**.
    - **Degree:** Set `degree=10` (Reduced from 12/16 to improve stability per Advisor history).
    - **Composition:** Pass `[Fourier(x_{norm}), t_{norm}]` to the Main Body.

**4.2. Main Body Architecture (KAN)**
- **Type:** **Chebyshev KAN**.
- **Scaling Law:**
    - Base MLP Width: 30.
    - KAN Degree (Polynomial order): 3.
    - **Instruction:** Set `width_KAN = 16` (approx $30 / \sqrt{3+1}$).
    - **Depth:** 3 layers.
    - **Learning Rate:** Set `lr_KAN = 0.0003` (Base LR / 3).

**4.3. Output Transformation (Hard Constraints)**
- **Ansatz:** Enforce Initial Condition exactly.
  $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, x_{embed}) $$
- **Periodicity:** Enforced by Fourier Features on $x$.

**5. Constraint Implementation Strategy**
- **IC:** Hard Constraint (Ansatz).
- **BCs:** Hard Constraint (Fourier Features).
- **PDE:** Soft Loss.
- **Entropy:** Soft Loss.

**6. Loss Function Assembly**
- **Symbolic:** $L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{entropy}$
- **Definitions:**
    - $L_{pde}$: MSE of $(u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx})$.
    - $L_{entropy}$: MSE of $\text{ReLU}(\partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3))$. (Penalize positive entropy production).

**6.1. Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
- **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
- **Phase 1 (Adam):** Static $\nu = 0.01$. Iterations: 10,000.
- **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** Annealing Schedule.
    - Sequence: $\nu = [0.005, 0.002, 0.001, 0.0005, 0.0001]$.
    - Dwell: Increase iterations for lower $\nu$ (e.g., 5k $\to$ 25k).
    - **CRITICAL:** Reinitialize Hessian at each step.

**7. Weighting Strategy**
- **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
- **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    - **Phase 1 (Adam):** **Self-Balancing** (GradNorm).
    - **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** global weights at final Adam values.

**8. Visualization Strategy**
- Standard plots. Ensure `u_pred` and `u_sol` are compared.

**9. Saving Data**
- Save `u_pred` and `u_sol` to `burgers_pred.npz`.

Main Model:
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]

Enhancements:
[Periodic Fourier Features, Chebyshev KAN]

--- Critic report---
[REJECTED]

**Critique:** Violates the “Inverse Exception” of the Reynolds/viscosity ladder: the plan states “ν is a learnable/annealed parameter” and simultaneously prescribes a fixed continuation schedule on ν (Phase 1 ν=0.01, then ν=[0.005,…,0.0001] in Phase 2). You cannot schedule continuation on a parameter that is being learned; ν must either be fixed and externally annealed, or treated as a trainable constant without a pre-set schedule.

**Requirement:** Decide on ν’s role and adjust the plan accordingly:
- **Option A (Recommended for this problem):** Treat ν as an external continuation parameter, **not** learnable. Remove “learnable” from the problem statement, keep the existing vanishing-viscosity schedule (ν=0.01 in Adam, then [0.005, 0.002, 0.001, 0.0005, 0.0001] with increasing dwell in SSBroyden).
- **Option B:** If you truly want ν to be learned, then:
  - Fix ν to a **single** (trainable) scalar with bounded parametrization (e.g., ν = ν_min + (ν_max−ν_min)·sigmoid(θ)),
  - **Remove** any continuation/annealing schedule on ν (no stepwise changes in ν across stages),
  - Use continuation only on other safe hyperparameters if needed (e.g., sampling density), but not on ν itself.


--- 🔄 Strategy Iteration 2/5 ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
Role: Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.

### Analysis Steps

**0. Triage - Determine Strategy**
- **User Request:** Solve 1D Inviscid Burgers' equation ($u_t + uu_x = 0$) with Periodic BCs.
- **Advisor Report:**
    - **Diagnosis:** Previous MLP models achieved high accuracy but suffered from instability (oscillations) in the final vanishing viscosity stages due to shock stiffness.
    - **Recommendation:** Switch Main Body to **Kolmogorov-Arnold Network (KAN)** using **Chebyshev polynomials**.
    - **Constraint:** **MUST** maintain **Periodic Fourier Features** on input $x$ to enforce periodicity.
    - **Optimality:** Use Entropy Penalty for shocks.
- **Critic Report:** Clarified that $\nu$ must be an *external continuation parameter*, not a learnable parameter.
- **Strategy:** Implement the Advisor's KAN architecture with the established Vanishing Viscosity continuation schedule.

**1. Data Source**
- **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
- **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.

**2. Problem Statement**
- **Governing Equation (Viscous Form for Continuation):**
  $$ u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx} = 0 $$
  (where $\nu$ is an external continuation parameter, NOT learnable).
- **Initial Condition:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
- **Boundary Conditions:** Periodic in $x$.
- **Entropy Condition (Inviscid Limit):**
  $$ \partial_t(u^2/2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) \le 0 $$
- **Problem Type:** Forward.
- **Well-Posedness:** Well-posed via Vanishing Viscosity method.

**2.1. Input**
- Domain: $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.

**2.2. Output**
- $u(t,x)$ (Scalar).
- Characteristics: Shock formation. High gradients.

**3. Base Model Selection**
- **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden. (Base template, modified to KAN).

**4. Main Architecture**
- **Structure:** Input $\to$ Normalization $\to$ Embeddings $\to$ **KAN Main Body** $\to$ Output Transform $\to$ Output.

**4.1. Input Transformations**
- **Normalization:**
    - $x_{norm} = x$ (already in $[-1, 1]$).
    - $t_{norm} = t$ (already in $[0, 1]$).
    - **CRITICAL:** Apply normalization *inside* the model's `forward` method.
- **Feature Embeddings:**
    - **Periodic Fourier Features:** Applied to $x_{norm}$ **ONLY**.
    - **Degree:** Set `degree=10` (Reduced from 12/16 to improve stability).
    - **Composition:** Pass `[Fourier(x_{norm}), t_{norm}]` to the Main Body.

**4.2. Main Body Architecture (KAN)**
- **Type:** **Chebyshev KAN**.
- **Scaling Law:**
    - Base MLP Width: 30.
    - KAN Degree (Polynomial order): 3.
    - **Instruction:** Set `width_KAN = 16` (approx $30 / \sqrt{3+1}$).
    - **Depth:** 3 layers.
    - **Learning Rate:** Set `lr_KAN = 0.0003` (Base LR / 3).

**4.3. Output Transformation (Hard Constraints)**
- **Ansatz:** Enforce Initial Condition exactly.
  $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, x_{embed}) $$
- **Periodicity:** Enforced by Fourier Features on $x$.

**5. Constraint Implementation Strategy**
- **IC:** Hard Constraint (Ansatz).
- **BCs:** Hard Constraint (Fourier Features).
- **PDE:** Soft Loss.
- **Entropy:** Soft Loss.

**6. Loss Function Assembly**
- **Symbolic:** $L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{entropy}$
- **Definitions:**
    - $L_{pde}$: MSE of $(u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx})$.
    - $L_{entropy}$: MSE of $\text{ReLU}(\partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3))$. (Penalize positive entropy production).

**6.1. Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
- **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
- **Phase 1 (Adam):** Static $\nu = 0.01$. Iterations: 10,000.
- **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** Annealing Schedule.
    - Sequence: $\nu = [0.005, 0.002, 0.001, 0.0005, 0.0001]$.
    - Dwell: Increase iterations for lower $\nu$ (e.g., 5k $\to$ 10k $\to$ 15k $\to$ 20k $\to$ 25k).
    - **CRITICAL:** Reinitialize Hessian at each step.

**7. Weighting Strategy**
- **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
- **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    - **Phase 1 (Adam):** **Self-Balancing** (GradNorm).
    - **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** global weights at final Adam values.

**8. Visualization Strategy**
- Standard plots. Ensure `u_pred` and `u_sol` are compared.

**9. Saving Data**
- Save `u_pred` and `u_sol` to `burgers_pred.npz`.

Main Model:
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]

Enhancements:
[Periodic Fourier Features, Chebyshev KAN]

--- Critic report---
[APPROVED]

Physics vs Strategy: Uses viscous Burgers with ν>0 plus vanishing-viscosity continuation and entropy penalty for the inviscid/shock case (meets zero-viscosity trap requirements); continuation is discrete, starts at ν=0.01 and ends at ν=1e-4 with increasing dwell and Hessian reinit at each step (SSBroyden mandate satisfied). Hierarchy & Authority: Follows advisor’s key instruction (switch to Chebyshev KAN with periodic Fourier features), keeps the exact data path and keys, treats ν as external (not learned), and solves the correct forward problem with the specified IC/BC; no forbidden variables are introduced. Constraints & BCs: No incompressible-flow or corner-singularity issues in 1D periodic Burgers; IC enforced via hard ansatz, periodicity via Fourier features is acceptable. Optimization & Weighting: Uses Adam → SSBroyden; global weights are self-balanced during Adam and explicitly frozen for SSBroyden, while local vRBA weights are used for weighting/sampling in Adam and (by prior template) for sampling only in SSBroyden; no weak formulation is introduced.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires two architectural enhancements. First, `Periodic_Fourier_Features` is used as an input embedding layer to enforce periodic boundary conditions. This class is self-contained. Second, the main model body is replaced with a `Chebyshev KAN`. This is implemented by the `KAN` class, which acts as a wrapper. When configured with `basis_type='chebyshev'`, the `KAN` class specifically instantiates and uses the `Polynomial_KAN_layer`. Therefore, both the `KAN` class and its dependency, `Polynomial_KAN_layer`, must be extracted.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

    ```python
        layers: Sequence[int]

            else:
                raise ValueError(f"Unsupported basis_type for this implementation: {self.basis_type}. Only 'chebyshev' is configured.")
        
        @nn.compact
        def __call__(self, x):
            for feat in self.layers:
                x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
            return x
    ```
*   **Action:** Replace the `MLP` class and the old `PINN` class (lines 12-44) with a new version of the `PINN` class. This new version removes the `MLP` and instantiates the `KAN` as its main body, accepting a `kan_degree` parameter as required by the scientific strategy.
*   **New Code (to replace lines 12-44):**
    ```python
    class PINN(nn.Module):
        features: Sequence[int]
        lb: jnp.ndarray
        ub: jnp.ndarray
        embedding_degree: int = 1
        kan_degree: int = 3
        Lx: float = 2.0
        
        def setup(self):
            self.enbedding = Periodic_Fourier_Features(kmax=self.embedding_degree, Lx=self.Lx)
            self.KAN = KAN(layers=self.features, degree=self.kan_degree, basis_type='chebyshev')

        @nn.compact
        def __call__(self, t: Array, x: Array) -> Array:
            # Normalize inputs
            t_norm = (t - self.lb[0]) / (self.ub[0] - self.lb[0])
            x_norm = 2.0 * (x - self.lb[1]) / (self.ub[1] - self.lb[1]) - 1.0
            
            x_embedded = self.enbedding(x_norm)
            X_concat = jnp.concatenate([t_norm, x_embedded], axis=-1)
            
            H = self.KAN(X_concat)
            
            # Apply hard constraint ansatz
            u = -jnp.sin(jnp.pi * x) + t * H
            return u
    ```

--- 6. Reconstructing Code ---
--- 4. Calling Debugging Team ---
Inspector summary:
 The implementation uses a PINN architecture featuring Periodic Fourier Features (degree=10) applied exclusively to normalized x inputs, concatenated with normalized t, feeding into a Chebyshev KAN main body with layers [16,16,16,1] (width=16, depth=3 hidden, polynomial degree=3), followed by the hard-constrained ansatz output u = -sin(πx) + t * NN(t, x_embed). Normalization occurs differentiably inside the model's forward method. The optimizer workflow is Hybrid: Phase 1 Adam for 10,000 iterations at fixed ν=0.01 with vRBA local weighting (exponential potential, EMA updates, importance sampling), self-balancing global weights via GradNorm (moving average of gradient norms) for L_pde and L_entropy; Phase 2 SSBroyden with vanishing viscosity continuation schedule ν=[0.005 (dwell 5000 iters), 0.002 (10000), 0.001 (15000), 0.0005 (20000), 0.0001 (25000)], Hessian reinitialized at each ν step, vRBA local weights updated per outer iteration and frozen global lamEnt from Adam phase end, using N_change=100 inner BFGS steps per outer iter with resampling. Loss terms exactly match L_total = L_pde (MSE of viscous Burgers residual u_t + ∂_x(0.5 u²) - ν u_xx with vRBA) + L_entropy (MSE of ReLU(∂_t(0.5 u²) + ∂_x(u³/3))).
Proposed name:Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX\Question :

$$ u_t + u u_x= 0 $$

### Strategy: 1D Inviscid Burgers (Shock Capturing via KAN & Continuation)

**1. Data Source**
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.
*   **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.

**2. Problem Statement**
*   **Governing Equation (Viscous Form for Continuation):**
    $$ u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx} = 0 $$
    *   *Note:* We solve the viscous form and anneal $\nu \to 0$ to capture the entropy solution of the inviscid problem.
*   **Entropy Condition (Auxiliary Residual):**
    $$ R_{ent} = \partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) \le 0 $$
*   **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
*   **IC:** $u(0,x) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
*   **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
*   **Problem Type:** Forward.
*   **Well-Posedness:** Weak solution with shocks; requires vanishing viscosity and entropy condition.

**3. Base Model Selection**
*   **Architecture:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.
*   **Enhancements:** [Periodic Fourier Features, KAN]

**4. Main Architecture**
*   **Structure:** Input ($t,x$) $\to$ Normalization $\to$ Embeddings $\to$ Main Body $\to$ Output Transformation $\to$ $u$.
*   **4.1 Input Transformations:**
    *   **Normalization:** Normalize $t \in [0,1]$ and $x \in [-1,1]$ **inside** the forward pass.
    *   **Embeddings:**
        *   **Periodic Fourier Features:** Apply **ONLY** to normalized $x$.
        *   **Degree:** Set `degree=10` (Global Stability Constraint).
        *   **Input Composition:** Pass `[t_norm, Fourier(x_norm)]` to the Main Body.
*   **4.2 Main Body (KAN Scaling):**
    *   **Advisor Instruction:** Switch to KAN.
    *   **Base MLP Specs:** Width=30, Depth=3.
    *   **KAN Derivation:**
        *   `width_KAN = int(30 / sqrt(3 + 1)) = 15`.
        *   `lr_KAN = Base_LR / 3`.
        *   Use **Chebyshev** basis (degree=3).
*   **4.3 Output Transformation (Hard Constraints):**
    *   **Ansatz:** $u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, x_{embedded})$.
    *   *Reasoning:* Enforces IC exactly at $t=0$. Enforces Periodicity exactly via Fourier features on $x$.

**5. Constraint Implementation Strategy**
*   **Count:** $L_{pde}$ (1) + $L_{entropy}$ (1) = 2 terms. ($L_{ic}$ and $L_{bc}$ are architectural).
*   **Strategy:** Loss-Based for PDE and Entropy; Architectural for IC/BC.

**6. Loss Function Assembly**
*   **Symbolic Formula:**
    $$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{entropy} $$
*   **Definitions:**
    *   $L_{pde}$: MSE of $(u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx})$.
    *   $L_{entropy}$: Mean of $(\text{ReLU}(R_{ent}))^2$. (Penalize positive entropy production).

**6.1 Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
*   **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
*   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Static $\nu = 0.01$. Iterations: 10,000.
*   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** Annealing Schedule.
    *   **Re-initialization:** Reset Hessian to Identity at each step.
    *   **Schedule:**
        1.  $\nu=0.005$, Dwell=5,000
        2.  $\nu=0.002$, Dwell=10,000
        3.  $\nu=0.001$, Dwell=15,000
        4.  $\nu=0.0005$, Dwell=20,000
        5.  $\nu=0.0001$, **Dwell=50,000** (Extended per Advisor).

**7. Weighting Strategy**
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Strategy:** Self-Balancing (GradNorm).
    *   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamic updates.
    *   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** weights at final Adam values.

**8. Visualization & Saving**
*   **Visualization:** Standard plots. Ensure $u(t,x)$ is plotted.
*   **Saving:** Save `u_pred` as `.npz` with keys matching reference data if possible, or standard keys.

The plan respects all checked constraints:  
- Physics & Reynolds/viscosity ladder: Inviscid Burgers is handled via viscous regularization (ν > 0) plus entropy penalty and a **discrete** vanishing-viscosity continuation (0.01 → 0.0001) with ≥5 steps, monotonically increasing dwell times, final step ≥25k (actually 50k), and **Hessian reinitialized** at each ν; no forbidden ν=0 strong-form.  
- Inverse-problem rules: Not an inverse problem; no forbidden continuation on unknowns or misuse of pressure/data.  
- Incompressible-flow constraint: Not applicable (1D Burgers, no incompressibility requirement).  
- Advisor & history compliance: Uses the exact data path and keys; problem is still forward Burgers with vanishing viscosity + entropy penalty and hybrid Adam/SSBroyden; advisor’s explicit request (increase final-stage dwell time) is obeyed by extending the last ν=1e-4 stage to 50k iterations; no weak formulation is introduced.  
- Continuation with SSBroyden: Parameters are piecewise constant per stage (no continuous ramp), ≥5 steps, increasing dwell, Hessian reset each stage, and global weights `lam` frozen before SSBroyden while local `lambdas` (vRBA) remain adaptive/sampling-based only in SSBroyden.  
- Boundary/IC handling & singularity: Periodic BCs and IC are enforced via hard ansatz with Fourier features; no incompatible corner singularities and no hard BC ansatz across conflicting values.  
- Data handling: Data is used only for validation/error metrics as specified; saving is noted, and there is no modification of user-provided paths or keys.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires two architectural enhancements. First, `Periodic_Fourier_Features` is used as an input embedding layer to enforce periodic boundary conditions. This class is self-contained. Second, the main model body is replaced with a `KAN` using a Chebyshev basis. This is implemented by the `KAN` class, which acts as a wrapper. When configured with `basis_type='chebyshev'`, the `KAN` class specifically instantiates and uses the `Polynomial_KAN_layer`. Therefore, both the `KAN` class and its dependency, `Polynomial_KAN_layer`, must be extracted.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

Debugger Status: REJECTED_FATAL
Debugger Analysis: **Cell 18 (SSBroyden Training Loop):** The code executed without crashing (exit code 0), but the **Log File reveals a catastrophic numerical instability** during the second-order optimization phase. This is a **fixable Planner bug**, not a user environment error.

**Evidence from Log:**
- The final SSBroyden iteration (nu=1.0e-04) shows:
  - `Loss=6.097e+247` (astronomically large)
  - `RL2=1.613e+61` (relative L2 error is 10^61, physically meaningless)
  - `lam_max=4.350e-39` (lambda weights collapsed to near-zero)
- The PDE residuals plotted in the final figure show: `Min: 1.23e+57, Max: 7.16e+61` (completely diverged).
- Despite this, the code claims a "Minimum achieved RL2 error: 0.003228..." in stdout, which is **inconsistent** with the training logs. This suggests the minimum was achieved *before* the SSBroyden phase, and the continuation schedule caused the solution to diverge.

**Root Cause Diagnosis:**
The continuation schedule in **Cell 18 (Lines 682-689)** aggressively reduces viscosity (`nu`) from 0.005 → 0.0001 over 100,000 iterations. For the inviscid Burgers equation with a shock-forming initial condition (`-sin(πx)`), this is a **known failure mode**:
1. As `nu → 0`, the PDE becomes hyperbolic and develops shocks.
2. The PINN's smooth neural network representation cannot capture discontinuities without specialized techniques (e.g., adaptive refinement near shocks, or a different ansatz).
3. The loss function explodes because the residuals at the shock become unbounded.

**Why This is a Planner Error (Not User Error):**
- The user requested solving the **inviscid** Burgers equation (`u_t + u u_x = 0`), but the Planner introduced a *viscous* term (`nu * u_xx`) and used continuation to reduce `nu`.
- While this is a valid strategy for some problems, the Planner failed to:
  1. Recognize that the initial condition (`-sin(πx)`) will form a shock at `t ≈ 1/π ≈ 0.318`.
  2. Implement safeguards (e.g., stopping the continuation if the loss diverges, or using entropy-stable schemes).
  3. The entropy residual term (Cell 7, Lines 310-325) was *intended* to stabilize shocks, but the self-balancing weight `lamEnt` (Cell 11, Line 416) clearly failed to prevent divergence.

**Specific Bug in Cell 18:**
- **Line 698-731:** The continuation loop does not check if the loss or error is increasing. It blindly continues reducing `nu` even after the solution has diverged.
- **Missing Logic:** After each `SSBroyden_step`, the code should compare `loss_val` or `error` to the previous iteration. If either increases by >10x (or exceeds a threshold like 1e10), the loop should either:
  - Revert to the previous `nu` value and reduce the step size, OR
  - Stop the continuation and return the best solution found so far.

**Recommended Fix:**
Add a divergence check in the SSBroyden loop (after Line 719):
```python
if loss_val > 1e10 or error > 1e10:
    print(f"Divergence detected at nu={nu}. Stopping continuation.")
    break
```
Alternatively, the Planner should reconsider the continuation schedule or use a more robust shock-capturing method (e.g., WENO-based PINNs or a characteristic-based ansatz).

  - [Planner]: Instructions received:
### Cell 18
*   **Action:** Replace the SSBroyden training loop (lines 16-50) to implement the critical divergence check mandated by the `[ERROR_REPORT]`. The original code failed to detect numerical instability during the viscosity continuation, leading to a diverged solution. The new code introduces a check after each optimization step; if the loss or relative L2 error exceeds a safe threshold (e.g., loss > 1e10 or error > 1.0), it prints a warning and halts the entire continuation schedule to prevent further corruption of the solution.
*   **New Code:**
    ```python
    # Outer loop for viscosity annealing
    diverged = False
    for step_info in continuation_schedule:
        nu = step_info['nu']
        dwell_time = step_info['dwell']
        num_outer_iterations = dwell_time // Nchange
        # Reinitialize Hessian at the start of each new nu step
        H0 = jnp.eye(len(initial_weights), dtype=jnp.float64)
        print(f"\nStarting continuation step with nu = {nu}, dwell time = {dwell_time} iterations.")
        pbar = tqdm(range(num_outer_iterations), desc=f"SSBroyden Training (nu={nu:.1e})")
        for it in pbar:
            initial_weights, H0, key, loss_val, nit, lambdas = SSBroyden_step(
                initial_weights, H0, key,
                tc, xc, ti, xi, ui, lambdas,
                apply_fn, unflatten_func, batch_size, gamma, eta, k_samp_bfgs, c_samp_bfgs,
                static_options_tuple, it, nu, lamEnt
            )
            effective_steps += nit
            current_params_for_eval = unflatten_func(initial_weights)
            u_pred_it = apply_fn(current_params_for_eval, t, x)
            error = relative_l2(u_pred_it, u_gt)

            # Divergence Check as per ERROR_REPORT
            if loss_val > 1e10 or jnp.isnan(loss_val) or error > 1.0:
                print(f"\nDivergence detected at nu={nu}. Loss: {loss_val:.2e}, Error: {error:.2e}. Halting continuation.")
                diverged = True
                break

            error_vec = u_pred_it - u_gt
            L_infty = scipy.linalg.norm(error_vec.flatten(), ord=np.inf)
            L_2 = scipy.linalg.norm(error_vec.flatten(), ord=2)
            all_errors.append(error)
            all_loss.append(loss_val)
            all_its.append(effective_steps + Adam_Steps)
            all_L_infty.append(L_infty)
            all_L_2.append(L_2)
            pbar.set_postfix({
                'It': effective_steps,
                'Loss': f'{loss_val:.3e}',
                'RL2': f'{float(error):.3e}',
                'nu': f'{nu:.1e}',
                'lam_max': f'{jnp.max(lambdas):.3e}',
            })
        if diverged:
            break
    ```

However, the final continuation step to $\nu=0.0001$ was too aggressive. The problem became too stiff, causing the optimizer to fail and the solution to diverge catastrophically. The underlying methodology (KAN, vRBA, Entropy Penalty) is excellent, but the continuation schedule was not gradual enough for this difficult regime.

*   **Targeted Improvements:**
    *   **For Integrity:** None.
    *   **For Accuracy:** The continuation step from $\nu=0.0005$ to $\nu=0.0001$ was too large, leading to divergence. The model was stable and accurate at the previous step. **Reduce the difficulty of the final continuation step:** Instead of jumping directly to $\nu=0.0001$, introduce a more gradual annealing schedule. For the next run, attempt an intermediate viscosity, such as **$\nu=0.0003$**, to approach the inviscid limit more carefully.

### 0. Triage - Determine Strategy
*   **Role:** Training Data (L_data included)? **No.**
*   **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY? **Yes.**
*   **Strategy:** **Path 2 (Advisor Recommendation).** The Advisor identifies the failure as a "Critical Instability" caused by an aggressive continuation schedule in the final step. The methodology (KAN, vRBA, Entropy) is validated.
*   **Mandatory Action:** Implement the **KAN** architecture with the **Entropy Penalty**, but insert an **intermediate viscosity step ($\nu=0.0003$)** in the continuation schedule to prevent divergence.

### 1. Data Source
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Keys:** `t` (200,), `x` (256,), `usol` (200x256).
*   **Instruction:** Load this data solely for computing validation error metrics (L2 Relative Error) during training.

### 2. Problem Statement
*   **Governing Equation (Viscous Form for Continuation):**
    $$ r_{pde} = u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx} $$
    *   *Note:* The target is inviscid ($\nu=0$), but we solve the viscous form with $\nu \to 0$.
*   **Entropy Condition (CRITICAL):**
    $$ r_{ent} = \text{ReLU}( \partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) ) $$
    *   *Note:* Enforces physical shock selection.
*   **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
*   **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
*   **ICs:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
*   **Problem Type:** Forward.
*   **Well-Posedness:** Well-posed for Viscous Burgers; Inviscid limit requires Entropy condition.

### 2.1 Input
*   **Domain:** 1D space + time ($d=2$).

### 2.2 Output
*   **Value:** Scalar $u(t,x)$.
*   **Characteristics:** Shock formation at $t \approx 1/\pi$. High-frequency features.

### 3. Base Model Selection
*   **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.
*   **Enhancements:** KAN, Periodic Fourier Features.

### 4. Main Architecture
*   **Structure:** Input ($t,x$) $\to$ Normalization $\to$ Embeddings ($x$ only) $\to$ KAN Main Body $\to$ Output Transform $\to$ $u$.

**4.1 Input Transformations**
*   **Normalization:** Normalize $t \in [0, 1]$ and $x \in [-1, 1]$ inside the forward pass.
*   **Feature Embeddings:**
    *   **Periodic Fourier Features:** Apply to normalized $x$ **ONLY**.
    *   **Degree:** 10.
    *   **Composition:** Concatenate `[t_norm, x_embedded]`.

**4.2 Main Body Architecture (KAN Scaling)**
*   **Base MLP Specs:** Width=30, Depth=3.
*   **KAN Derivation:**
    *   **Width:** `width_KAN = int(30 / sqrt(3 + 1)) = 15`.
    *   **Depth:** 3 layers.
    *   **Basis:** Chebyshev (Degree 3).
    *   **Learning Rate:** `lr_KAN = Base_LR / 3`.

**4.3 Output Transformation (Ansatz)**
*   **Goal:** Enforce IC and Periodic BCs (via features).
*   **Ansatz:**
    $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, x_{embed}) $$
*   **Logic:** At $t=0$, $u = -\sin(\pi x)$. Periodicity is handled by the Fourier features on $x$.

### 5. Constraint Implementation Strategy
*   **Count:** `L_pde` + `L_entropy` = 2 terms. (BCs/ICs handled by architecture).
*   **Strategy:** **Loss-Based** for PDE and Entropy.

### 6. Loss Function Assembly
*   **Symbolic Formula:**
    $$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{entropy} $$
*   **Definitions:**
    *   `L_pde`: MSE of $r_{pde}$ (weighted by vRBA).
    *   `L_entropy`: MSE of $r_{ent}$ (weighted by global `lam_ent`).

**6.1 Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
*   **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
*   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Static $\nu = 0.01$. Dwell 10,000 iters.
*   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** Annealing Schedule.
    *   $\nu = 0.005$ (Dwell 5,000)
    *   $\nu = 0.002$ (Dwell 10,000)
    *   $\nu = 0.001$ (Dwell 15,000)
    *   $\nu = 0.0005$ (Dwell 20,000)
    *   **$\nu = 0.0003$ (Dwell 20,000)**  <-- **CRITICAL ADVISOR FIX**
    *   $\nu = 0.0001$ (Dwell 30,000)
*   **Instruction:** Reinitialize Hessian to identity at each $\nu$ step.

### 7. Weighting Strategy
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for `L_pde`.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Strategy:** **Self-Balancing (GradNorm)**.
    *   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamically update `lam_pde` and `lam_entropy`.
    *   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** global weights at final Adam values.

### 8. Visualization Strategy
*   Standard plots. Ensure `u_pred` vs `u_ref` is plotted at final time $t=1$.

### 9. Saving Data
*   Save `u_pred` field as `.npz`.

--- Critic report---
[APPROVED]

All required checks are satisfied:

- **Physics/Formulation & Viscosity (Constraint E & Reynolds ladder):** Uses viscous Burgers with ν>0 and a vanishing-viscosity continuation down to ν=1e-4 (not ν=0), plus entropy penalty for shock selection; no inviscid-strong-form trap.
- **Inverse/Parameters (Constraint B):** Forward problem only; no inverse parameters or forbidden variables, so no sigmoid/secondary network issues.
- **Continuation Strategy (Constraint D):** Parameter (ν) is piecewise constant per stage with discrete steps; ≥5 steps; dwell times non-decreasing with the hardest step (ν=1e-4) at 30k iterations; Hessian explicitly reinitialized at each ν.
- **Boundary/IC Treatment (Constraint A & Singularity Check):** No incompressible flow; 1D Burgers. IC enforced via hard ansatz; periodic BCs naturally supported by Fourier features, no conflicting corner singularities or improper hard constraints.
- **Advisor Compliance & Data Integrity (Hierarchy & Authority):** Advisor’s fix (insert ν=0.0003 intermediate stage) is explicitly implemented; data path and keys match the user’s exactly; data used only for validation, not in L_data.
- **Parameter Definition Audit:** All physical constants are either specified (viscosity schedule values) or clearly described as continuation parameters; target is inviscid limit via ν→0, not symbolic tautologies.
- **Optimization & Weighting (Stability Mandate):** Global weights (lam_pde, lam_entropy) are self-balanced during Adam and **frozen** during SSBroyden; vRBA `lambdas` used for PDE weighting/sampling (local) and are not frozen globally.
- **No Weak Formulations:** Plan uses strong-form residuals only, with no weak/variational formulation introduced.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires two architectural enhancements. First, `Periodic_Fourier_Features` is used as an input embedding layer to enforce periodic boundary conditions. This class is self-contained. Second, the main model body is replaced with a `KAN` using a Chebyshev basis. This is implemented by the `KAN` class, which acts as a wrapper. When configured with `basis_type='chebyshev'`, the `KAN` class specifically instantiates and uses the `Polynomial_KAN_layer`. Therefore, both the `KAN` class and its dependency, `Polynomial_KAN_layer`, must be extracted.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

--- 🔄 Strategy Iteration 1/5 ---
--- Strategy Report Received ---
### Analysis Steps

**0. Triage - Determine Strategy**
- **User Request:** Solve 1D Inviscid Burgers' equation.
- **Advisor Report:** The previous run (Trial 15) was highly accurate (0.06% error) but unstable in the final convergence phase.
- **Mandatory Action:** Follow the Advisor's recommendation to **increase the dwell time of the final continuation step ($\nu=0.0001$) by at least 50%** (from 30k to 50k iterations). Maintain the KAN architecture and Entropy regularization.

**1. Data Source**
- **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.
- **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
- **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.

**2. Problem Statement**
- **Governing Equation:** Vanishing Viscosity Burgers' Equation (to approximate Inviscid limit).
  $$ r_{pde} = u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx} $$
  *Note: $\nu$ is a learnable/annealed parameter, not a fixed constant.*
- **Entropy Condition (Regularization):**
  $$ r_{ent} = \text{ReLU}( \partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) ) $$
- **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
- **IC:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
- **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
- **Problem Type:** Forward (Vanishing Viscosity).

**2.1. Input**
- Domain dimension: 1D space + time ($x, t$).

**2.2. Output**
- Value dimension: Scalar $u(t,x)$.
- Characteristics: Shock formation at $x=0$, steep gradients.

**3. Base Model Selection**
- **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.
- **Enhancements:** KAN, Periodic Fourier Features.

**4. Main Architecture**
- **Structure:** Input $\to$ Normalization $\to$ Embeddings $\to$ KAN Main Body $\to$ Ansatz $\to$ Output.

**4.1. Input Transformations**
- **Normalization:** Normalize $t \in [0, 1]$ and $x \in [-1, 1]$ *inside* the forward pass.
- **Feature Embeddings:**
  - **Type:** **Periodic Fourier Features**.
  - **Application:** Apply **ONLY** to normalized $x$. Pass normalized $t$ raw.
  - **Specs:** `degree=10` (Global Stability Constraint).

**4.2. Main Body Architecture (KAN)**
- **Advisor Instruction:** Switch to KAN.
- **Base MLP Specs:** `width=30`, `depth=3`.
- **Derived KAN Specs:**
  - `width_KAN = 16` (approx $30 / \sqrt{3+1}$).
  - `grid_size = 5` (standard KAN grid).
  - `spline_order = 3`.

**4.3. Output Transformation (Ansatz)**
- **Goal:** Enforce IC and Periodic BCs hard constraints.
- **Ansatz Formula:**
  $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{KAN}(t, x_{embed}) $$
- **Logic:**
  - At $t=0$, $u = -\sin(\pi x)$ (Exact IC).
  - Since $x_{embed}$ uses Periodic Features and $\sin(\pi x)$ is periodic, the output is strictly periodic in $x$ for all $t$.

**5. Constraint Implementation Strategy**
- **IC:** Hard Constraint (Ansatz).
- **BCs:** Hard Constraint (Periodic Features).
- **PDE:** Loss-based ($L_{pde}$).
- **Entropy:** Loss-based ($L_{ent}$).

**6. Loss Function Assembly**
- **Symbolic Formula:**
  $$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{ent} $$
- **Terms:**
  - $L_{pde}$: MSE of $u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx}$.
  - $L_{ent}$: MSE of $r_{ent}$ (Entropy residual).

**6.1. Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
- **Phase 1: Adam (Stabilization)**
  - $\nu = 0.01$ (Static).
  - Iterations: 10,000.
- **Phase 2: SSBroyden (Annealing)**
  - **Schedule:**
    1. $\nu=0.005$, Dwell=5,000.
    2. $\nu=0.002$, Dwell=10,000.
    3. $\nu=0.001$, Dwell=15,000.
    4. $\nu=0.0005$, Dwell=20,000.
    5. $\nu=0.0003$, Dwell=20,000.
    6. $\nu=0.0001$, **Dwell=50,000** (Advisor Mandate: +50% increase).
  - **Instruction:** Reinitialize Hessian to identity at each step.

**7. Weighting Strategy**
- **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
  - Adam: Update weights & use for sampling.
  - SSBroyden: Use for sampling ONLY (weights frozen or updated per outer loop, but NO gradient flow to weights).
- **Global Weights (`lam`):**
  - **Strategy:** Self-Balancing (GradNorm).
  - **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamic updates for `lam_pde` and `lam_ent`.
  - **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** global weights at final Adam values.

**8. Visualization Strategy**
- Standard plots. Ensure `u_pred` is plotted against `usol`.

**9. Saving Data**
- Save `u_pred` to `burgers.npz` with keys `t`, `x`, `u_pred`.

Main Model:
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]
Enhancements:
[Periodic Fourier Features, KAN]

--- Critic report---
[REJECTED]

**Critique:** Violates the Reynolds/viscosity ladder and the inverse-parameter exception. The problem is stated as *forward* vanishing-viscosity with a prescribed continuation schedule, but you simultaneously describe ν as “learnable/annealed,” which is an inverse setup. For inverse problems, continuation/annealing on the unknown parameter is forbidden; ν must either be fixed per stage (pure forward curriculum, not learned) or treated as a learnable scalar without a pre-imposed schedule, but not both. Also, target ν=0.0001 corresponds to Re≈10⁴, where entropy regularization AND viscosity AND continuation are required and correctly present, but your “ν learnable” wording conflicts with the mandated fixed, piecewise-constant ν per continuation step.

**Requirement:**  
Clarify ν’s role and fix the continuation logic:

1. **Choose one:**
   - **Forward problem (recommended here):** Treat ν as a *fixed, known* scalar at each stage of the continuation (0.01 → … → 0.0001). Remove “ν is learnable” everywhere; ν must be a constant hyperparameter per stage, not a trained parameter.
   - **Inverse problem:** If ν is truly unknown and learned, **remove the manual continuation schedule on ν entirely** and let gradient descent learn ν directly; do not anneal ν.

2. Keep the existing vanishing-viscosity schedule only in the **forward** interpretation (piecewise-constant ν, Hessian reinit each step, final ν=1e-4 with 50k SSBroyden iters as Advisor requested).

3. Update the problem statement and loss definitions to reflect the chosen option unambiguously, then re-submit the strategy.

- **Advisor Report:** Previous run (Trial 15) achieved high accuracy (0.06%) but lacked stability in the final convergence phase.
- **Mandatory Action:** Follow Advisor's instruction to **increase the dwell time of the final continuation step ($\nu=0.0001$) to 50,000 iterations** (previously 30k).
- **Critic Correction:** Treat $\nu$ strictly as a **fixed hyperparameter** that changes per stage (Forward Vanishing Viscosity), NOT as a learnable parameter.

**1. Data Source**
- **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.
- **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
- **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.

**2. Problem Statement**
- **Governing Equation:** Vanishing Viscosity Burgers' Equation.
  $$ r_{pde} = u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx} $$
  *Note: $\nu$ is a fixed scalar constant provided by the continuation schedule.*
- **Entropy Condition (Regularization):**
  $$ r_{ent} = \text{ReLU}( \partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) ) $$
- **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
- **IC:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
- **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
- **Problem Type:** Forward (Vanishing Viscosity).

**2.1. Input**
- Domain dimension: 1D space + time ($x, t$).

**2.2. Output**
- Value dimension: Scalar $u(t,x)$.
- Characteristics: Shock formation at $x=0$.

**3. Base Model Selection**
- **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.
- **Enhancements:** KAN, Periodic Fourier Features.

**4. Main Architecture**
- **Structure:** Input $\to$ Normalization $\to$ Embeddings $\to$ KAN Main Body $\to$ Ansatz $\to$ Output.

**4.1. Input Transformations**
- **Normalization:** Normalize $t \in [0, 1]$ and $x \in [-1, 1]$ *inside* the forward pass.
- **Feature Embeddings:**
  - **Type:** **Periodic Fourier Features**.
  - **Application:** Apply **ONLY** to normalized $x$. Pass normalized $t$ raw.
  - **Specs:** `degree=10` (Global Stability Constraint).

**4.2. Main Body Architecture (KAN)**
- **Advisor Instruction:** Use KAN (proven effective in Trial 15).
- **Base MLP Specs:** `width=30`, `depth=3`.
- **Derived KAN Specs:**
  - `width_KAN = 16` (approx $30 / \sqrt{3+1}$).
  - `grid_size = 5`.
  - `spline_order = 3`.

**4.3. Output Transformation (Ansatz)**
- **Goal:** Enforce IC and Periodic BCs hard constraints.
- **Ansatz Formula:**
  $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{KAN}(t, x_{embed}) $$
- **Logic:**
  - At $t=0$, $u = -\sin(\pi x)$ (Exact IC).
  - Since $x_{embed}$ uses Periodic Features and $\sin(\pi x)$ is periodic, the output is strictly periodic in $x$ for all $t$.

**5. Constraint Implementation Strategy**
- **IC:** Hard Constraint (Ansatz).
- **BCs:** Hard Constraint (Periodic Features).
- **PDE:** Loss-based ($L_{pde}$).
- **Entropy:** Loss-based ($L_{ent}$).

**6. Loss Function Assembly**
- **Symbolic Formula:**
  $$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{ent} $$
- **Terms:**
  - $L_{pde}$: MSE of $u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx}$.
  - $L_{ent}$: MSE of $r_{ent}$ (Entropy residual).

**6.1. Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
- **Phase 1: Adam (Stabilization)**
  - $\nu = 0.01$ (Fixed).
  - Iterations: 10,000.
- **Phase 2: SSBroyden (Annealing)**
  - **Schedule:**
    1. $\nu=0.005$, Dwell=5,000.
    2. $\nu=0.002$, Dwell=10,000.
    3. $\nu=0.001$, Dwell=15,000.
    4. $\nu=0.0005$, Dwell=20,000.
    5. $\nu=0.0003$, Dwell=20,000.
    6. $\nu=0.0001$, **Dwell=50,000** (Advisor Mandate).
  - **Instruction:** Reinitialize Hessian to identity at each step.

**7. Weighting Strategy**
- **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
  - Adam: Update weights & use for sampling.
  - SSBroyden: Use for sampling ONLY (weights frozen or updated per outer loop, but NO gradient flow to weights).
- **Global Weights (`lam`):**
  - **Strategy:** Self-Balancing (GradNorm).
  - **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamic updates for `lam_pde` and `lam_ent`.
  - **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** global weights at final Adam values.

**8. Visualization Strategy**
- Standard plots. Ensure `u_pred` is plotted against `usol`.

**9. Saving Data**
- Save `u_pred` to `burgers.npz` with keys `t`, `x`, `u_pred`.

Main Model:
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]
Enhancements:
[Periodic Fourier Features, KAN]

--- Critic report---
[APPROVED]

Physics vs Strategy: Uses vanishing-viscosity Burgers with ν>0 and continuation down to ν=1e-4 (Re≈1e4) plus entropy regularization, satisfying the Reynolds/viscosity ladder and zero-viscosity trap; ν is explicitly treated as a fixed, piecewise-constant hyperparameter per stage (not learned), with discrete continuation steps and Hessian reinit each step.  

Hierarchy & Authority: Fully respects the Advisor’s instruction (only change is increasing final ν=1e-4 dwell to 50k iters), keeps the same KAN + entropy setup, uses the exact user data path and keys, and all symbols (ν values, domain, IC, BCs) are numerically and correctly specified; data is used only for validation (no forbidden uses).  

Constraints & Singularities: No incompatible boundary values meet at corners in this 1D periodic setting, so the hard IC ansatz and periodicity via Fourier features are acceptable; no global hard BC ansatz conflict.  

Optimization & Weighting: Follows Hybrid Adam → SSBroyden with self-balancing global weights in Adam, then explicitly freezes global weights in SSBroyden; vRBA local `lambdas` are used for weighting+sampling in Adam and sampling-only in SSBroyden, without being frozen, preserving the Hessian integrity and respecting the continuation rules (≥5 steps, final 50k iters).

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires two architectural enhancements. First, `Periodic_Fourier_Features` is used as an input embedding layer to enforce periodic boundary conditions. This class is self-contained. Second, the main model body is replaced with a `KAN` using a Chebyshev basis. This is implemented by the `KAN` class, which acts as a wrapper. When configured with `basis_type='chebyshev'`, the `KAN` class specifically instantiates and uses the `Polynomial_KAN_layer`. Therefore, both the `KAN` class and its dependency, `Polynomial_KAN_layer`, must be extracted.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

**Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback**
*   **Observation:**
    1.  **Loss & Error History:** The training exhibits a clear continuation pattern with sharp drops in loss and error at each new viscosity stage. The model achieves a minimum relative L2 error of approximately $6 \times 10^{-4}$ during the final stage. However, this stability is short-lived. In the final stage (starting around 70,000 iterations), the error and loss become highly erratic and then diverge catastrophically, with the final L2 error spiking to over 1.0.
    2.  **Residual/Pointwise Error:** The PDE residual and pointwise error heatmaps show that the largest errors are concentrated along the shock front ($x=0$ for $t>0.3$), which is expected. However, the magnitude of the PDE residual reaches over $10^2$, indicating a complete failure to satisfy the governing equation in the stiff region.
    3.  **Solution Fields:** The predicted solution is a heavily smoothed, inaccurate representation of the reference. It completely fails to capture the sharp shock discontinuity, instead presenting a blurry and oscillatory field that does not match the ground truth physics.

*   **Diagnosis:** **Continuation Failure / Numerical Instability.** The model successfully navigated the initial, higher-viscosity stages of the curriculum. However, the transition to the final, most challenging stage ($\nu=0.0001$) proved too abrupt. The problem became too stiff for the optimizer to handle, leading to a catastrophic numerical instability and divergence. The strategy to increase the number of iterations for this final stage, intended to improve convergence, instead provided more time for the instability to manifest and grow, ultimately causing the training to fail.

*   **Targeted Improvements:**
    *   **For Integrity:** None
    *   **For Accuracy:** The continuation step from $\nu=0.0003$ to $\nu=0.0001$ is too aggressive, causing the divergence. Refine the continuation schedule by inserting an intermediate viscosity step to make the transition more gradual. **Recommendation: Add a training stage with $\nu=0.0002$ before attempting the final $\nu=0.0001$ stage.**
    *   **For Details:** None
    *   **For Optimality:**
        *   *Constraint Formulation:* The current formulation is excellent. For future reference on other problems: For 2D NS, use Stream Function ($\psi$). For 3D NS, use Vector Potential ($\vec{A}$). This reduces unknowns and enforces $\nabla \cdot u = 0$ exactly.
        *   *Output Transforms:* For problems with positive quantities (e.g., Density/Pressure), enforce positivity via an Output Transform: $u = \text{Softplus}(\text{Net}(x)) + \epsilon$.
        *   *Entropy (FOR SHOCKS - CRITICAL):* The use of an Entropy Penalty and conservative form is correct and was critical for getting this far. This should be considered mandatory for all inviscid/low-viscosity problems with shocks.

--- Strategy Report Received ---
### Analysis Steps

**1. Data Source**
*   **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.

**2. Problem Statement**
*   **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers' Equation (for continuation).
    $$ r_{pde} = u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx} $$
    *   Note: $\nu$ is a learnable/annealed parameter, not a fixed constant.
*   **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
*   **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
*   **ICs:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
*   **Problem Type:** Forward.
*   **Well-Posedness:** Well-posed for $\nu > 0$. Requires vanishing viscosity strategy for inviscid limit.

**2.1 Input**
*   Domain: 1D space ($x$) + time ($t$).

**2.2 Output**
*   $u(t,x)$ (Scalar).
*   Characteristics: Shock formation at $x=0$.

**3. Base Model Selection**
*   **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden.

**4. Main Architecture**
*   **Structure:** Input $\rightarrow$ Normalization $\rightarrow$ Embeddings $\rightarrow$ Main Body $\rightarrow$ Output Transformation $\rightarrow$ Output.

**4.1 Input Transformations**
*   **Normalization:** Normalize $t$ to $[0, 1]$ and $x$ to $[-1, 1]$ *inside* the forward pass.
*   **Feature Embeddings:**
    *   **Periodic Fourier Features:** Apply to normalized $x$ **ONLY**.
    *   **Degree:** 10.
    *   **Pass-through:** Concatenate embeddings of $x$ with raw normalized $t$.

**4.2 Main Body Architecture**
*   **Type:** **Chebyshev KAN**.
*   **Specs:**
    *   Width: 16 (Derived from Base MLP 30).
    *   Depth: 3 hidden layers.
    *   Polynomial Degree: 3.
    *   Grid Size: 5.

**4.3 Output Transformation**
*   **Ansatz (Hard Constraints):**
    $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, x_{embed}) $$
    *   Enforces IC exactly at $t=0$.
    *   Enforces Periodicity exactly via Periodic Fourier Features on $x$.

**5. Constraint Implementation Strategy**
*   **Dirichlet/IC:** Enforced via Architectural Ansatz (Hard Constraints).
*   **Periodicity:** Enforced via Input Embedding (Hard Constraints).
*   **Soft Losses:** None required for BC/IC.

**6. Loss Function Assembly**
*   **Symbolic Formula:**
    $$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + L_{entropy} $$
*   **Components:**
    *   **$L_{pde}$:** MSE of $u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx}$.
    *   **$L_{entropy}$:** Entropy condition for shock capturing.
        $$ L_{entropy} = \text{MSE}\left( \text{ReLU}\left( \partial_t\left(\frac{u^2}{2}\right) + \partial_x\left(\frac{u^3}{3}\right) \right) \right) $$

**6.1 Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
*   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Static $\nu = 0.01$. Iterations: 10,000.
*   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** Annealing Schedule.
    *   **Re-initialization:** Reset Hessian to Identity at each step.
    *   **Schedule:**
        1.  $\nu=0.005$ (5,000 iters)
        2.  $\nu=0.002$ (10,000 iters)
        3.  $\nu=0.001$ (15,000 iters)
        4.  $\nu=0.0005$ (20,000 iters)
        5.  $\nu=0.0003$ (20,000 iters)
        6.  **$\nu=0.0002$ (20,000 iters)** (MANDATORY INTERMEDIATE STEP)
        7.  $\nu=0.0001$ (50,000 iters)

**7. Weighting Strategy**
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
    *   Adam: Used for Weighting & Sampling.
    *   SSBroyden: Used for Sampling ONLY.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Strategy:** Self-Balancing (GradNorm).
    *   **Adam:** Dynamic updates for $L_{pde}$ and $L_{entropy}$.
    *   **SSBroyden:** **FREEZE** weights at final Adam values.

**8. Visualization Strategy**
*   Standard plots. Do not modify templates.

**9. Saving Data**
*   Save `u_pred` and `u_exact` to `burgers.npz`.

Main Model:
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]
Enhancements:
[Chebyshev KAN, Periodic Fourier Features]

It also corrupts data integrity by overwriting the user’s reference file path/name (`burgers.npz`) instead of treating it as read‑only validation data.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires two architectural enhancements. First, `Periodic_Fourier_Features` is used as an input embedding layer to enforce periodic boundary conditions. This class is self-contained. Second, the main model body is replaced with a `Chebyshev KAN`. This is implemented by the `KAN` class, which acts as a wrapper. When configured with `basis_type='chebyshev'`, the `KAN` class specifically instantiates and uses the `Polynomial_KAN_layer`. Therefore, both the `KAN` class and its dependency, `Polynomial_KAN_layer`, must be extracted.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT

**Section 1: Status Decision**
*   **Status:** `REJECTED_FOR_REFINEMENT`

**Section 2: Quantitative Scorecard**
*   **Score Integrity:** 5
*   **Score Accuracy:** 15
*   **Score Details:** 0
*   **Score Optimality:** 15
*   **TOTAL SCORE:** 35

**Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback**
*   **Observation:**
    1.  **Loss & Error History:** The training exhibits a clear continuation learning pattern, with both loss and L2 error decreasing stepwise through several stages. The model achieves a minimum relative L2 error of approximately $10^{-3}$. However, at the final continuation stage (around 70,000 iterations), both the loss and error catastrophically diverge. The L2 error explodes from $10^{-3}$ to over $2.0$ (200%), and the loss becomes extremely erratic.
    2.  **Residual/Pointwise Error:** The PDE residual plot shows extremely large values (up to $10^7$), indicating a complete failure to satisfy the governing equation at the end of the training. The pointwise error is large across the entire domain, not just localized at the shock.
    3.  **Solution Fields:** The predicted solution is corrupted by severe, high-frequency, unphysical oscillations across the entire domain for $t>0.2$. It fails to capture the characteristic shock formation of the Burgers' equation and bears no resemblance to the smooth reference solution.

*   **Diagnosis:** **Continuation Failure / Divergence.** The model and training strategy are methodologically excellent, successfully reducing the error through multiple stages of the vanishing viscosity curriculum. However, the final step in the schedule, reducing the viscosity to $\nu=0.0001$, was too aggressive. The problem became too stiff for the optimizer to handle, leading to a catastrophic numerical instability and the complete collapse of the solution. The log confirms this explicitly: `Divergence detected at nu=0.0001. Loss: 1.56e-02, Error: 2.10e+00. Halting continuation.`

*   **Targeted Improvements:**

    *   **For Integrity:** None. The code correctly identified the divergence and halted gracefully.
    *   **For Accuracy:** The physical formulation (conservative form, entropy penalty) is correct and state-of-the-art. The instability is due to the continuation schedule. **Recommendation: Make the continuation schedule less aggressive.** The model was stable before the final step. Modify the schedule to terminate at the last known stable viscosity ($\nu=0.0002$) to secure a valid, albeit slightly more viscous, solution. Alternatively, introduce smaller intermediate steps (e.g., $\nu=0.00015$) to bridge the gap to the final target.
    *   **For Details:** None. The primary issue is the catastrophic divergence, which must be addressed first.
    *   **For Optimality:** None. The chosen methodology (Chebyshev KAN, Periodic Fourier Features, vRBA, Entropy Penalty, SSBroyden) is optimal for this class of problem. The failure was in parameter tuning, not method selection.

--- Strategy Report Received ---
### Analysis Steps

**0. Triage - Determine Strategy**
- **Strategy:** Path 2 (Advisor Recommendation).
- **Advisor Instruction:** The previous run failed due to aggressive continuation ($\nu=0.0001$). The Advisor explicitly recommends terminating the schedule at the last known stable viscosity ($\nu=0.0002$) to ensure stability and accuracy.
- **Action:** I will implement the Chebyshev KAN architecture with Periodic Fourier Features and a robust Vanishing Viscosity continuation schedule ending strictly at $\nu=0.0002$.

**1. Data Source**
- **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.
- **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
- **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.

**2. Problem Statement**
- **Governing Equation (Viscous Burgers):**
  $$ r_{pde} = u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx} $$
  (Note: We solve the viscous form and anneal $\nu \to 0$).
- **Entropy Condition (Lax-Oleinik):**
  $$ r_{ent} = \text{ReLU}\left( \partial_t\left(\frac{u^2}{2}\right) + \partial_x\left(\frac{u^3}{3}\right) \right) $$
- **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
- **IC:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
- **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
- **Problem Type:** Forward.
- **Well-Posedness:** Well-posed for $\nu > 0$.

**2.1 Input**
- Domain dimension: 1D space + time ($d=2$).

**2.2 Output**
- Value dimension: Scalar $u(t,x)$.
- Characteristics: Shock formation at $x=0$, smooth elsewhere.

**3. Base Model Selection**
- **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.
- **Enhancement:** Chebyshev KAN (Advisor confirmed optimality).

**4. Main Architecture**
- **Structure:** Input $\to$ Normalization $\to$ Embeddings $\to$ Main Body $\to$ Output Transformation $\to$ Output.

**4.1 Input Transformations**
- **Normalization:** Normalize $t$ to $[0, 1]$ and $x$ to $[-1, 1]$ *inside* the forward pass.
- **Feature Embeddings:**
  - **Type:** **Periodic Fourier Features** (Option A).
  - **Application:** Apply **ONLY** to normalized $x$. Pass normalized $t$ raw.
  - **Specs:** `degree=10` (Stable configuration).
  - **Composition:** Concatenate `[Fourier(x), t]`.

**4.2 Main Body Architecture (KAN Scaling)**
- **Base MLP Specs:** `width=30`, `depth=3`.
- **KAN Derivation:**
  - Polynomial Degree: 3.
  - `width_KAN` = $30 / \sqrt{3+1} = 15 \to$ Set to **16**.
  - `lr_KAN` = Base_LR / 3.
- **Layer Type:** Chebyshev KAN Layer.

**4.3 Output Transformation (Ansatz)**
- **Goal:** Enforce IC and Periodic BCs strictly.
- **Ansatz:**
  $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, x_{embed}) $$
- **Logic:**
  - At $t=0$, $u = -\sin(\pi x)$ (Exact IC).
  - Periodicity is enforced by the Fourier features on $x$.

**5. Constraint Implementation Strategy**
- **Count:** $L_{pde}$ (1) + $L_{ent}$ (1). (IC and BCs are hard-constrained).
- **Strategy:** Loss-Based for PDE and Entropy; Architectural for IC/BC.

**6. Loss Function Assembly**
- **Symbolic Formula:**
  $$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + \lambda_{ent} L_{ent} $$
- **Components:**
  - $L_{pde}$: MSE of $r_{pde}$ (Viscous Burgers).
  - $L_{ent}$: MSE of $r_{ent}$ (Entropy residual).
  - $L_{ic}, L_{bc}$: **EXCLUDED** (Satisfied by Ansatz).

**6.1 Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
- **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
- **Phase 1 (Adam):** Static $\nu = 0.01$. Iterations: 10,000.
- **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** Annealing Schedule.
  - **Reinitialization:** Reset Hessian to identity at each step.
  - **Schedule (Conservative per Advisor):**
    1. $\nu=0.005$ (Dwell: 5,000)
    2. $\nu=0.002$ (Dwell: 10,000)
    3. $\nu=0.001$ (Dwell: 15,000)
    4. $\nu=0.0005$ (Dwell: 20,000)
    5. $\nu=0.0003$ (Dwell: 20,000)
    6. **$\nu=0.0002$ (Dwell: 30,000)** [FINAL TARGET]

**7. Weighting Strategy**
- **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
  - Adam: Use for Weighting & Sampling.
  - SSBroyden: Use for **Sampling ONLY**.
- **Global Weights (`lam`):**
  - **Strategy:** Self-Balancing (GradNorm).
  - **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamic updates for $\lambda_{ent}$ (keep $\lambda_{pde}=1$).
  - **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** $\lambda_{ent}$ at the final Adam value.

**8. Visualization Strategy**
- Standard plots. Ensure `u_pred` and `u_ref` are compared.

**9. Saving Data**
- Save `u_pred`, `x`, `t` to `burgers_pred.npz`.

**List ONLY specialized architectural modules/layers:**
[Periodic Fourier Features, Chebyshev KAN]

--- Critic report---
[REJECTED]

**Critique:** Violates the Reynolds/viscosity continuation bounds: the plan caps the vanishing-viscosity schedule at ν=2×10⁻⁴, which corresponds to Re≈5000 (<10⁴), while the rules require the continuation target to reach approximately inviscid conditions with Re_target ≥10⁴, i.e., ν_target ≤10⁻⁴. Advisor’s recommendation to stop at ν=2×10⁴ cannot override this global constraint.

**Requirement:** Adjust the continuation schedule so that the final stage reaches ν≈1×10⁻⁴ (or smaller, but not zero), with piecewise-constant ν per stage, at least 5 steps, monotonically increasing dwell times, and ≥25,000 iterations at the final ν stage.
