--- Critic report---
[APPROVED]

Physics vs Strategy: Uses viscous Burgers with ν>0 and vanishing-viscosity continuation (not ν=0), includes entropy residual, respects the zero-viscosity trap by keeping ν_final=1e-4 with continuation. Reynolds/viscosity ladder: Start ν=0.01 and anneal down in discrete, piecewise-constant steps to ν=1e-4 (Re≈1e4), ≥5 stages, final dwell=30k iterations, and no continuation on an unknown parameter. Continuation with SSBroyden: Hessian is explicitly reinitialized at each ν step; all ν changes are discrete between stages (no continuous ramping). Data & hierarchy: Path and keys copied exactly from USER_QUESTION, used only for validation as required; Advisor’s request to make the last step less aggressive is honored via added bridge step while still reaching ν=1e-4 to satisfy global constraints. Constraints & BCs: Forward problem, no inverse parameters, no forbidden variables; periodicity enforced via Fourier features plus hard IC ansatz, no misuse of weak forms. Optimization & weighting: Hybrid Adam→SSBroyden with global weights frozen before SSBroyden, local vRBA lambdas used for weighting+sampling in Adam and sampling-only in SSBroyden, matching the Hessian-integrity rules.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires two architectural enhancements. First, `Periodic_Fourier_Features` is used as an input embedding layer to enforce periodic boundary conditions. This class is self-contained. Second, the main model body is replaced with a `Chebyshev KAN`. This is implemented by the `KAN` class, which acts as a wrapper. When configured with `basis_type='chebyshev'`, the `KAN` class specifically instantiates and uses the `Polynomial_KAN_layer`. Therefore, both the `KAN` class and its dependency, `Polynomial_KAN_layer`, must be extracted.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

**Section 3: Forensic Analysis & Feedback**

    4.  **Error Distribution:** The pointwise error and PDE residual plots correctly identify the shock front (around x=0) as the primary source of error, which is expected for this problem.

*   **Diagnosis:** **High-frequency oscillations and convergence instability in the final low-viscosity stage.** The model architecture and physics formulation are excellent, leading to a highly accurate solution. However, as the viscosity ($\nu$) was annealed to a very small value, the problem became extremely stiff. The SSBroyden optimizer, while powerful, began to struggle, leading to an unstable search for the minimum. The model found a good solution but could not converge stably.

*   **Targeted Improvements:**
    *   **For Integrity:** None
    *   **For Accuracy:** The primary bottleneck is optimization stability, not model capacity or formulation. **Increase the number of iterations (dwell time) for the final, most challenging continuation stages (e.g., for $\nu \le 0.0005$).** The current dwell times are insufficient for the optimizer to dampen oscillations and settle into a stable minimum in this highly stiff regime. Doubling the iterations for the last two stages is a reasonable starting point.
    *   **For Details:** The main goal for the next run is to achieve a flat, stable plateau in the loss and error curves during the final training phase. The oscillations must be eliminated to ensure a robustly converged solution.
    *   **For Optimality:** None. The current methodology (KAN, Fourier Features, Entropy Penalty, vRBA, SSBroyden) is state-of-the-art for this problem.

--- Strategy Report Received ---
### Strategy: 1D Inviscid Burgers' Equation (Refined Continuation)

**1. Data Source**
*   **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.

**2. Problem Statement**
*   **Governing Equation (Viscous Form for Continuation):**
    $$ u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2) - \nu u_{xx} = 0 $$
    *   *Note:* We solve the viscous form and anneal $\nu \to 0$ to approach the inviscid limit while managing shock formation.
*   **Entropy Condition (Auxiliary Loss):**
    To ensure the correct physical shock solution (Lax entropy condition):
    $$ \partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) \le 0 $$
    *   *Implementation:* Minimize $L_{ent} = \text{MSE}(\text{ReLU}(\text{LHS}))$.
*   **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
*   **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
*   **IC:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
*   **Problem Type:** Forward. Well-posed with entropy condition.

**3. Base Model Selection**
*   **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.
*   **Architecture Mode:** **KAN** (Advisor Requirement: "Current methodology is state-of-the-art").

**4. Main Architecture**
*   **Structure:** `Input -> Normalization -> [t_norm, Fourier(x_norm)] -> KAN Body -> Ansatz -> Output`
*   **4.1 Input Transformations:**
    *   **Normalization:** Normalize $t \in [0, 1]$ and $x \in [-1, 1]$ **inside** the forward pass.
    *   **Embeddings:**
        *   **Periodic Fourier Features:** Apply to normalized $x$ **ONLY**.
        *   **Degree:** 10.
        *   **Composition:** Concatenate `t_norm` (raw) with `Fourier(x_norm)`.
*   **4.2 Main Body (KAN Scaling):**
    *   **Base MLP Specs:** Width 30, Depth 3.
    *   **KAN Specs:**
        *   `width_KAN = 16` (approx $30 / \sqrt{3+1}$).
        *   `depth = 3` hidden layers.
        *   `basis_degree = 3` (Chebyshev).
        *   `lr_KAN = Base_LR / 3`.
*   **4.3 Output Transformation (Hard Constraints):**
    *   **Ansatz:** Enforce IC exactly. Periodicity is enforced by Fourier features on $x$.
        $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, x_{embed}) $$

**5. Constraint Implementation Strategy**
*   **IC:** Hard Constraint (Ansatz).
*   **BCs:** Hard Constraint (Fourier Features).
*   **Loss Terms:** $L_{pde}$, $L_{ent}$.
*   **Count:** 2 terms.

**6. Loss Function Assembly**
$$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + \lambda_{ent} L_{ent} $$
*   **$L_{pde}$:** MSE of Residual ($u_t + u u_x - \nu u_{xx}$).
*   **$L_{ent}$:** MSE of Entropy Residual ($\text{ReLU}(\partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3))$).

**6.1 Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
*   **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
*   **Phase 1 (Adam):**
    *   $\nu = 0.01$ (Static).
    *   Iterations: 10,000.
*   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden - Stabilized Schedule):**
    *   **Advisor Instruction:** "Double iterations for $\nu \le 0.0005$."
    *   **Schedule:**
        1.  $\nu=0.005$, Dwell=5,000
        2.  $\nu=0.002$, Dwell=10,000
        3.  $\nu=0.001$, Dwell=15,000
        4.  $\nu=0.0005$, Dwell=**40,000** (Doubled)
        5.  $\nu=0.0002$, Dwell=**40,000** (Doubled)
        6.  $\nu=0.0001$, Dwell=**50,000** (Target)
    *   **Critical:** Reinitialize Hessian to Identity at each $\nu$ step.

**7. Weighting Strategy**
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA**.
    *   **Adam:** Used for Weighting & Sampling.
    *   **SSBroyden:** Used for **Sampling ONLY**.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):** Use **Self-Balancing (GradNorm)**.
    *   **Adam:** Dynamic updates for $\lambda_{ent}$ (keep $\lambda_{pde}=1$).
    *   **SSBroyden:** **FREEZE** $\lambda_{ent}$ at the final Adam value.

**8. Visualization & Saving**
*   **Save:** `burgers.npz` with keys `t`, `x`, `u_pred`.
*   **Plots:** Standard validation plots.

Main Model:
[MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBrodyden]
Enhancements:
[Periodic Fourier Features, KAN]

--- Critic report---
[APPROVED]

Physics & formulation: Uses viscous Burgers with ν→0 continuation plus entropy penalty for correct inviscid limit; includes mandatory small viscosity and continuation for shock problem, with piecewise-constant ν per stage.  
Reynolds/continuation ladder: Starts at ν=0.01 and anneals down to 1e-4 in ≥5 discrete steps; dwell times are monotonically increasing and final stage has 50k iters; Hessian is reinitialized each ν-step.  
Inverse/stability constraints: Forward (not inverse) problem; no forbidden variables; no continuation on unknowns.  
Boundary/IC handling: IC enforced via hard ansatz; periodicity handled through Fourier features; no conflicting corner BCs, so hard constraints are acceptable.  
Advisor compliance: Follows advisor’s instruction to increase dwell time for ν≤5e-4 and keeps methodology (KAN, Fourier, entropy, vRBA, SSBroyden).  
Optimization & weighting: Adam → SSBroyden with global weight λ_ent self-balanced then frozen before SSB, local vRBA lambdas used for weighting+sampling in Adam and sampling-only in SSB; no weak-form shortcut.  
Data integrity: Uses the exact user path and keys for read-only validation; predictions saved to a separate `burgers_pred.npz` file, avoiding overwrite.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires two architectural enhancements. First, `Periodic_Fourier_Features` is used as an input embedding layer to enforce periodic boundary conditions. This class is self-contained. Second, the main model body is replaced with a `Chebyshev KAN`. This is implemented by the `KAN` class, which acts as a wrapper. When configured with `basis_type='chebyshev'`, the `KAN` class specifically instantiates and uses the `Polynomial_KAN_layer`. Therefore, both the `KAN` class and its dependency, `Polynomial_KAN_layer`, must be extracted.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

Additionally, increase the number of iterations (dwell time) for these final, low-viscosity stages to give the optimizer more time to settle in the increasingly stiff energy landscape.

--- Strategy Report Received ---
### Strategy

**1. Data Source**
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.

**2. Problem Statement**
*   **Governing Equation (Viscous Form for Continuation):**
    $$ u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2) = \nu u_{xx} $$
    *   **Residual:** `r_pde = u_t + (0.5 * u**2)_x - nu * u_xx`
    *   **Entropy Condition (Inviscid Limit):**
        $$ \partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) \le 0 $$
    *   **Entropy Residual:** `r_ent = (0.5 * u**2)_t + (u**3 / 3)_x`
*   **Problem Type:** Forward.
*   **Well-Posedness:** Standard PINNs fail on shocks; we use **Vanishing Viscosity** ($\nu \to 0$) combined with **Entropy Regularization**.

**2.1 Input**
*   Domain: $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$. (1D+t).

**2.2 Output**
*   $u(t,x)$ (Scalar).
*   Characteristics: Shock formation at $x=0, t \approx 1/\pi$. High gradients.

**2.3 Analytical Solution**
*   Provided in `burgers.npz` key `'usol'`.

**3. Base Model Selection**
*   **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.

**4. Main Architecture**
*   **Structure:** Input $\to$ Input Transformation $\to$ Main Body $\to$ Output Transformation $\to$ Output.

**4.1 Input Transformations**
*   **Normalization:** Normalize $t$ to $[0, 1]$ and $x$ to $[-1, 1]$ **inside** the forward pass.
*   **Feature Embeddings:**
    *   **Periodic Fourier Features:** Apply to normalized $x$ **ONLY**.
    *   **Degree:** 10.
    *   **Composition:** Concatenate `[t_norm, embed(x_norm)]`. Do not pass raw $x$.

**4.2 Main Body Architecture (Advisor: Switch to KAN)**
*   **Base MLP Specs:** Width=30, Depth=3.
*   **KAN Scaling:**
    *   `width_KAN = int(30 / sqrt(3 + 1)) = 15` (Use 16).
    *   `lr_KAN = Base_LR / 3`.
*   **Specs:** Chebyshev KAN, Width=16, Depth=3, Polynomial Degree=3.

**4.3 Output Transformation (Hard Constraints)**
*   **Ansatz:** Enforce IC $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$ and Periodicity (via embeddings).
    $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, x_{embed}) $$
*   **Logic:** At $t=0$, $u = -\sin(\pi x)$. Periodicity is handled by the Fourier features on $x$.

**5. Constraint Implementation Strategy**
*   **Strategy:** **Architectural (Hard Constraints)** for IC and BCs.
*   **Loss Terms:**
    *   `L_pde`: Viscous Burgers residual.
    *   `L_ent`: Entropy regularization `mean(relu(r_ent)**2)`.

**6. Loss Function Assembly**
$$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + \lambda_{ent} L_{ent} $$
*   **Note:** `L_ic` and `L_bc` are excluded (satisfied by ansatz/architecture).

**6.1 Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
*   **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
*   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Static $\nu = 0.01$. Iterations: 10,000.
*   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** Dynamic $\nu$. **Reinitialize Hessian** at each step.
*   **Schedule (Advisor Refined):**
    1.  $\nu=0.005$, Dwell=5,000
    2.  $\nu=0.002$, Dwell=10,000
    3.  $\nu=0.001$, Dwell=15,000
    4.  $\nu=0.0005$, Dwell=20,000
    5.  **$\nu=0.00035$, Dwell=20,000** (Intermediate Step)
    6.  $\nu=0.0002$, Dwell=30,000 (Increased Dwell)
    7.  $\nu=0.0001$, Dwell=50,000 (Target)

**7. Weighting Strategy**
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):** Use **vRBA** for `L_pde`.
    *   **Adam:** Use for Weighting & Sampling.
    *   **SSBroyden:** Use for **Sampling ONLY**.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Strategy:** **Self-Balancing (GradNorm)**.
    *   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Dynamic updates for $\lambda_{ent}$ (keep $\lambda_{pde}=1$).
    *   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** **FREEZE** $\lambda_{ent}$ at final Adam value.

**8. Visualization Strategy**
*   Standard 1D+t plots. Do not modify template.

**9. Saving Data**
*   Save `u_pred` as `burgers_pred.npz` with keys `t`, `x`, `u`.

**List ONLY specialized architectural modules/layers:**
[Periodic Fourier Features, KAN]

--- Critic report---
[APPROVED]

The plan respects:  
- **Physics & Stability:** Uses viscous Burgers with vanishing-viscosity continuation (ν>0, down to 1e-4) plus entropy penalty for shocks; no ν=0; continuation is in **discrete steps** with ν fixed per step; at least 5 stages; final dwell (50k) ≥ 25k; includes an extra ν=0.00035 step and enlarged dwell at small ν per Advisor.  
- **Advisor Instructions:** Directly implements the Advisor’s fix (more gradual continuation between 0.0005 and 0.0002, increased dwell), keeps conservative form + entropy penalty + hard IC ansatz + Fourier features/KAN as previously successful.  
- **User & Data Integrity:** Solves the requested 1D inviscid Burgers via vanishing viscosity; uses the exact user-provided data path and keys solely for validation; no forbidden variables.  
- **Formulation & BCs:** Forward problem, no incompressibility constraints; hard IC via ansatz, periodicity via Fourier features—not via incorrect hard BCs on potentials.  
- **Continuation & SSBroyden:** ν-annealing is piecewise-constant per phase; Hessian is explicitly reinitialized at each ν step; dwell times are non-decreasing with a sufficiently long final step.  
- **Optimization & Weights:** Uses vRBA local weights (`lambdas`) for weighting+sampling in Adam and sampling-only in SSBroyden; global λ_ent is dynamically adapted in Adam and **frozen** for SSBroyden, preserving Hessian integrity.  
- **Constraints & Singularities:** No conflicting corner BCs; hard ansatz is appropriate.  
- **Saving:** Explicitly saves prediction in NPZ with `{t, x, u}` as required.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires two architectural enhancements. First, `Periodic_Fourier_Features` is used as an input embedding layer to enforce periodic boundary conditions. This class is self-contained. Second, the main model body is replaced with a `Chebyshev KAN`. This is implemented by the `KAN` class, which acts as a wrapper. When configured with `basis_type='chebyshev'`, the `KAN` class specifically instantiates and uses the `Polynomial_KAN_layer`. Therefore, both the `KAN` class and its dependency, `Polynomial_KAN_layer`, must be extracted.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

### 0. Triage - Determine Strategy
*   **Strategy:** **Refinement (Advisor-Driven)**.
*   **Advisor Recommendation:** The previous run (Trial 21) was excellent (Score 97) but showed incomplete convergence in the final stage. The Advisor explicitly requests increasing the dwell time for the final viscosity step ($\nu=0.0001$) from 50,000 to **80,000** iterations.
*   **Action:** Replicate the successful architecture (Chebyshev KAN) and methodology of Trial 21, modifying **only** the final step of the continuation schedule.

### 1. Data Source
*   **Role:** Validation/Error Metrics ONLY.
*   **Path:** `/users/jdtoscan/data/jdtoscan/LLM/Crunch_LLM/Results/NM_codes/Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX/2025_11_22_1730/burgers.npz`
*   **Keys:** `t`, `x`, `usol`.

### 2. Problem Statement
*   **Governing Equation:** Viscous Burgers' Equation (Conservative Form) used as a vanishing viscosity approximation for the Inviscid problem.
    $$ u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2) - \nu u_{xx} = 0 $$
    *   **Note:** $\nu$ is a trainable parameter controlled by the continuation schedule.
*   **Entropy Condition (Crucial for Shocks):**
    $$ \partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3) \le 0 $$
*   **Domain:** $x \in [-1, 1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
*   **BCs:** Periodic in $x$.
*   **IC:** $u(x,0) = -\sin(\pi x)$.
*   **Problem Type:** Forward (Solving from IC/BCs).
*   **Well-Posedness:** Hyperbolic conservation law developing shocks. Requires vanishing viscosity and entropy constraints for the unique physical solution.

### 3. Base Model Selection
*   **Model:** MLP with Hybrid Adam and SSBroyden.
*   **Architecture Mode:** **Chebyshev KAN** (per Trial 21 success).

### 4. Main Architecture
*   **Structure:** Input $\rightarrow$ Normalization $\rightarrow$ Feature Embedding $\rightarrow$ KAN Main Body $\rightarrow$ Output Transformation $\rightarrow$ Output.

**4.1. Input Transformations**
*   **Normalization:**
    *   $t \in [0, 1]$ (No change needed, but ensure bounds).
    *   $x \in [-1, 1]$ (No change needed).
    *   **CRITICAL:** Apply normalization *inside* the forward pass.
*   **Feature Embeddings:**
    *   **Periodic Fourier Features:** Applied to $x$ **ONLY**.
    *   **Degree:** 10.
    *   **Input to Network:** Concatenate `[t, Fourier(x)]`.

**4.2. Main Body Architecture (KAN)**
*   **Type:** Chebyshev KAN.
*   **Scaling:** Based on Base MLP (width=30).
    *   `width_KAN` = 16.
    *   `depth` = 3 hidden layers.
    *   `polynomial_degree` = 3.

**4.3. Output Transformation (Ansatz)**
*   **Goal:** Enforce IC and Periodic BCs hard.
*   **Ansatz:**
    $$ u_{pred}(t,x) = -\sin(\pi x) + t \cdot \text{NN}(t, \text{Fourier}(x)) $$
    *   *Explanation:* At $t=0$, $u = -\sin(\pi x)$. Periodicity is enforced by the Fourier features on $x$.

### 5. Constraint Implementation Strategy
*   **IC:** Hard-constrained via Ansatz.
*   **BCs:** Periodic Hard-constrained via Fourier Features.
*   **Dirichlet:** None required explicitly.
*   **Loss-Based Constraints:** PDE Residual and Entropy Condition.

### 6. Loss Function Assembly
*   **Symbolic Formula:**
    $$ L_{total} = L_{pde} + \lambda_{ent} L_{ent} $$
*   **Definitions:**
    *   $L_{pde}$: MSE of $(u_t + \partial_x(0.5 u^2) - \nu u_{xx})$.
    *   $L_{ent}$: MSE of $\text{ReLU}(\partial_t(0.5 u^2) + \partial_x(u^3/3))^2$.
    *   *Note:* $L_{ent}$ penalizes violations of the entropy condition.

**6.1. Continuation Strategy (Vanishing Viscosity)**
*   **Parameter:** $\nu$ (Viscosity).
*   **Phase 1 (Adam):** Static $\nu = 0.01$.
*   **Phase 2 (SSBroyden):** Annealing Schedule.
    *   **CRITICAL:** Reinitialize Hessian to Identity at each step.
    *   **Schedule:**
        1.  $\nu=0.005$, Dwell=5,000
        2.  $\nu=0.002$, Dwell=10,000
        3.  $\nu=0.001$, Dwell=15,000
        4.  $\nu=0.0005$, Dwell=20,000
        5.  $\nu=0.00035$, Dwell=20,000
        6.  $\nu=0.0002$, Dwell=30,000
        7.  $\nu=0.0001$, **Dwell=80,000** (Increased per Advisor).

### 7. Weighting Strategy
*   **Local Weights (`lambdas`):**
    *   Use **vRBA** for $L_{pde}$.
    *   **Adam:** Use for Weighting & Sampling.
    *   **SSBroyden:** Use for **Sampling ONLY**.
*   **Global Weights (`lam`):**
    *   **Strategy:** Self-Balancing (GradNorm).
    *   **Adam:** Dynamic updates for $\lambda_{ent}$ (keep $\lambda_{pde}=1$).
    *   **SSBroyden:** **FREEZE** $\lambda_{ent}$ at the final Adam value.

### 8. Visualization Strategy
*   Standard plots. Ensure `u` is saved.

### 9. Saving Data
*   Save `u_pred`, `x`, `t` as `burgers_pred.npz`.

**Explanation:**
The strategy requires two architectural enhancements. First, `Periodic_Fourier_Features` is used as an input embedding layer to enforce periodic boundary conditions. This class is self-contained. Second, the main model body is replaced with a `Chebyshev KAN`. This is implemented by the `KAN` class, which acts as a wrapper. When configured with `basis_type='chebyshev'`, the `KAN` class specifically instantiates and uses the `Polynomial_KAN_layer`. Therefore, both the `KAN` class and its dependency, `Polynomial_KAN_layer`, must be extracted.

**IMPLEMENTATION CODE BLOCK FOR PLANNER:**

### [Periodic_Fourier_Features]
```python
class Periodic_Fourier_Features(nn.Module):
    kmax: int = 1
    Lx: float = 2.0 
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X: Array) -> Array:
        ks = jnp.arange(1, self.kmax + 1)
        Xper = 2 * jnp.pi * jnp.matmul(X, ks[None, :]) / self.Lx
        xcos = jnp.cos(Xper)
        xsin = jnp.sin(Xper)
        Xper = jnp.concatenate([xcos, xsin], axis=1)
        return Xper
```

### [Polynomial_KAN_layer]
```python
class Polynomial_KAN_layer(nn.Module):
    out_dim: int
    degree: int
    polynomial_type: str='T' # T for Chebyshev and L is for Legendre
    normalization: callable = jnp.tanh
    def setup(self):
        self.T_funcs = [globals()[f"{self.polynomial_type}{i}"] for i in range(self.degree + 1)]
        self.normalization_fn=self.normalization
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, X):
        X = self.normalization_fn(X)
        in_dim = X.shape[1]
        # Initialize trainable parameters C_n
        C_n = self.param('C_n', nn.initializers.normal(1 / (in_dim * (self.degree + 1))),
                        (in_dim, self.out_dim, (self.degree + 1)))  # Shape: (In, Out, Degree+1)
        
        # Compute polynomial functions T_n
        T_n = jnp.stack([func(X) for func in self.T_funcs], axis=1)  # Shape: (Batch, Degree+1, In)
        
        # Reshape and transpose tensors for matrix multiplication
        T_n = T_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(X.shape[0], -1)      # Shape: (Batch, In * (Degree+1))
        C_n = C_n.transpose(0, 2, 1).reshape(-1, self.out_dim)    # Shape: (In * (Degree+1), Out)
        
        # Perform matrix multiplication
        C_n_Tn = T_n @ C_n  # Shape: (Batch, Out)
        return C_n_Tn
```

### [KAN]
```python
class KAN(nn.Module):
    layers: Sequence[int]
    degree: int = 5
    basis_type: str = 'chebyshev' 
    
    def setup(self):
        # Map basis_type to the correct configuration
        if self.basis_type.lower() == 'rbf': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = RBF_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev_grid': # KAN with RBF Radial basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'sin': # KAN with sin series basi function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Sin_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree}
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'chebyshev':# KAN with Chebyshev basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'T'} 
        elif self.basis_type.lower() == 'legendre': # KAN with RBF Legendre basis function layers
            self.basis_layer_class = Polynomial_grid_KAN_layer
            self.basis_kwargs = {'degree': self.degree, 'polynomial_type': 'L'}
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown basis_type: {self.basis_type}. Use 'chebyshev', 'legendre', or 'rbf'.")
    
    @nn.compact
    def __call__(self, x):
        for feat in self.layers:
            x = self.basis_layer_class(out_dim=feat, **self.basis_kwargs)(x)   
        return x
```

Inspector summary:
 Chebyshev KAN architecture with layers [16,16,16,1], polynomial degree 3; inputs normalized inside forward pass, Periodic Fourier Features (degree 10) applied to x only, concatenated with t; hard ansatz u = -sin(πx) + t * NN(t, Fourier(x)) enforcing IC and periodic BCs. Hybrid optimizer: Adam phase (10k epochs, vRBA for PDE weighting/sampling + GradNorm self-balancing for λ_ent); SSBroyden phase with vRBA sampling only, λ_ent frozen at Adam final value, Hessian reinitialized to identity per nu step; continuation schedule: ν=0.01 (Adam static), then ν=[0.005:5k, 0.002:10k, 0.001:15k, 0.0005:20k, 0.00035:20k, 0.0002:30k, 0.0001:80k] iterations (Nchange=100 steps per outer iter). Loss: L_pde (MSE viscous Burgers res w/ vRBA) + λ_ent L_ent (MSE ReLU(entropy res)^2).
Proposed name:Burgers_Periodic_SinPiX\Question :
Let's solve the 1D Inviscid Burgers' equation:
$$ u_t + u u_x= 0 $$

Domain: $x \in [-1,1]$, $t \in [0, 1]$.
BCs: Periodic in x.

Initial Condition:
$$ u(x,y,0) = -\sin(pi x)$$
